Modern History

(1707 AD - 1947 AD)

  • Transition Summary from Medieval to Modern

    • Age of Rajputs (7th Century to 13th Century)

      They Emerged After decline of Harsha Empire

    • Muslim Invasion from NWFP

      • Mohammad Bin Qasim - 712
        • He Plundered Sindh and Multan
      • Mahmood of Ghazni - 1000 - 1027
        • His Goal was to NOT establish a Islmaic Empire but to LOOT India
        • He was never defeated, of his invasions were successfull
      • Muhammad of Ghouri - 1175 - 1206
        • Didn't had any child, hence was surrounded by Slaves and Soldiers. One of his Slaves was Qutubuddin Aibak

    • Establishment of “Delhi Sultanate” (1206 - 1526)

      💡 एक SLAVE ने KHILJI को TUGHLAQI आदेश दिया की SAYYID को LODHI बना दे !

      They were mainly Turkish in Origin

    • Problems with Empire (Common with All Big Empires)

      • Communication
        1. was not Fast
        2. Means of Communication were - Pigeons and Horses
      • Troop Mobilisation Takes Time

    • Rise of Regional Local Powers - Specifically Maratha

      • Basics of Maratha

        1. Originally were Agricultural Peasants
        2. Used to Work in Bahamani Kingdom of Bijapur
        3. Good in Military Skills - Particularly Horse Riding
        4. Many Marathas became Military Personnel like Shah Ji Bhonsle

      • Shahaji Bhonsle - 2 Sons

        • Son 2 - Venko Ji Raje Bhonsle - Thanjur Guy
          1. Shah Ji other Son went on to rule territories in modern day Tamil Nadu’s Eastern Coastal Area (Thanjur)
          2. They are called Marathas of Thanjur
          3. Cultural Contribution of Marathas of Thanjur in Important for Exam
        • Son 1 - Shiva Ji Rao - Rebellious Guy - Part 1

          • Basic History

            1. Shah Ji + Wife Jija Bai = Shiva Ji (Born in Shivneri / Junnar )
            2. Because Shahjahan annexed Ahmednagar Shah Ji Bhonsle Lost Job
            3. Shah Ji Shifted to Bijapur → and then to → Modern Day Bangalore
            4. Meanwhile Shivaji and Jija Bai Lived in Poona
            5. Dadoji Kondadeo was appointed by Shah Ji Bhonsle for their
              1. Safety
              2. Tutoring and Mentoring

          • Military Skills of Shiva Ji Rao

            1. Dadoji Kondadeo taught Warfare to Shiva Ji - Especially Guerrilla Warfare
            2. Guerrilla Warfare - Irregular Warfare System
              1. Primary Deploy Hit and Run Mobility
              2. Hide and Attack Tactics,
              3. Including Ambushes, Sabotages, Raids
              4. Petty Warfare
            3. But Shiva Ji wasn't allowed to use his skills for his safety by Dadoji
            4. but Dadoji Kondadeo Died in 1664

          • Goals of Shivaji

            1. Defeat Muslim Empires in North India and Mughal Emperors
            2. Establish Hindu Empire

            Couldn't Establish His Goals becasye

            1. Aurangzeb was Governor of Ahmed Nagar
            2. Luck Favoured Shiva Ji
            3. Aurangzeb was forced to go to North India, because Shahjahan Fell ill

      • Background of Mughal Empire CONTEMPORARY to MARATHAS

        1. Shahjahan Most Favourite Son was Darashiko, he wanted him to be the next ruler
        2. Historic Battle - Battle of Samugarh, 1658 - Aurangzeb v/s Darashiko
        3. Aurangzeb Imprisoned Shahjahan in Under House Arrest in 1658 for 8 Years, Died in 1666 → Aurangzeb became Emperor of Mughal Emperor
        💡 ***Rule of Primogeniture*** - Eldest Son of a King Succeeds his father (Fratricidal Wars)
        1. Mughals Didn't follow the rule of Primogeniture

      • Son 1 - Shiva Ji Rao - Rebellious Guy - Part 2 (Afzal Shaista Shaista Raja Jai Singh)

        1. Now that Aurangzeb is in North India Until 1666
        2. Shiva Ji got Options of Implementing his Goals
        3. Afzal Khan - Military Commander of Bijapur Died 1659 Killed by Shiva Ji ( Weapon : Baghnag) #Clash 1 - Mughal v/s Maratha - Maratha Win
        4. Bijapur paid Tribute to Shiva Ji, Bijapur was Now with Marathas
        1. Aurangzeb now appointed Shaista Khan as Governor in South

        #Clash 2 - Mughal v/s Maratha - Mughal Win

        1. He Won Poona couldn't hold it because he was attacked by Shiva Ji in the night, Hence Shaista Khan had to run for his life

        #Clash 3 - Mughal v/s Maratha - Maratha Win

        1. Aurangzeb Appointed Raja Jai Singh in as Governor in 1663

        #Clash 4 - Mughal v/s Maratha - Mughal Win

        1. Raja Jai Singh Defeated Shivaji & “Treaty of Purandar 1665”
        • Treaty of Purandar
          1. Treaty of Purandar - Agreed to 3 things
            1. Surrender 23 Forts to Mughals
            2. To Pay Tribute to Mughals
            3. Pay his Respects to Aurangzeb in Mughal Court along with his Son Shambha Ji
              1. Aurangzeb had promised Raja Jai Singh that he will not kill Shiva Ji hence Shiva Ji went to Mughal Court
              2. Shiva Ji was made MANSABDAR and was made to wait in Long Queue along with others MANSABDARS, he started shouting
              3. Aurangzeb ordered Shiva Ji and his son was Imprisoned
        • Shiva Ji’s Imprisonment and Escape
          1. Imprisoned in Agra Fort
          2. Made Up a Stomach Problem
          3. Mughal Doctors were sent, Aurangzeb sent Money to Shiva Ji
          4. He bought Mangoes and Gifted it to Doctor and Servants
          5. Initially Checked and then there was leniency
          6. Shivaji Hid in Mangoes Basket and Ran Away
        • Shiva Ji’s Vision of Being a King
          1. Worked for 8 Years & convinced a Brahman Priest for Coronation
          2. being a Peasant he couldn't be the King
          3. due to Coronation Chhatrapati Shivaji became King of area around Poona and Maharashtra
          4. Created Navy to Counter Portuguese - Also Called Father of Indian Navy
        • Shiva Ji Summarised

          1. Founder of Maratha Empire

          2. Greatest Guerrilla Warfare Expert

          3. Founder of Indian Navy

            Tamil Word - Navay means Naval Vessels used by Chola Kings Raja Raja 1 and Rajendra 1

          4. Council of 8 Minister - ASHTHPRADHAN

            1. Prime Minister

              Sub Ordinate of Chhatrapati ,

              Could Guide but Not Over Ride his Decisions

            2. Samatya

            3. Sachiv

            4. Mantri

            5. Senapati

            6. Sumant

            7. Nyaydhish

            8. Pandit Rao

        Shiva Ji Natural Death in 1680

      • Shambha Ji

        1. Aurangzeb was waiting for an Opportunity
        2. Battle of Sangameshwar, 1689
          1. Shambha Ji Lost
          2. Tortured to death by Aurangzeb
            1. Didn't reveal details about a Hidden Treasure
            2. Refused converting to Islam
        3. Shambha Ji Family was Imprisoned by Aurangzeb

      • Sons of Shambha Ji

        • First Son - Shahu - Currently Imprisoned
          • Imprisoned with Others
        • Second Son - RajaRam
          1. managed to Escape to South India via Karnataka to JINJEE in 1689
          2. His Relatives were “Marathas of Thanjur”
          3. RajaRam + Tara Bai = Shiva Ji II
          4. Mughals attacked JINJEE in 1698

      • Contemporary Mughal Act

        1. Aurangzeb Wanted to remove Maratha from Indian Polity and wanted to Defeat Marathas
        2. Hence He Deployed Ghazi Firoz Jung and Zulfiqar Khan to Siege Deccan, Mughals Won
        3. RajaRam had Escaped from Jinjee to a hilly area in Maharashtra called Satara and he started ruling surrounding territories
        4. RajaRam Died in 1700 AD - Chain Smoker, Travel, Bad Health

      • Shiva Ji 2 - 5 Year Kid

        1. RajaRams Young Son Shivaji 2 became next Chhatrapati at the age of 5 Years
        2. Whenever There were issues with Kings like Young Age & ill health, Regents were appointed to rule on their behalf
        3. Tara Bai - Regent - Ruled over Satara for Next 7 Years (1707), in the Name of Shiva Ji 2
      💡 Meanwhile Aurangzeb Died in 1707 - Bahadur Shah came to Power - Released Shahu
      • Shahu Fought with { Shiva Ji 2 and Tara Bai }
        1. Battle of Khed 1707 - Shahu (Winner) v/s TaraBai
        2. Tara Bai Fled to Kolhapur
        3. Became Ruler of Satara & Chhattrapati (1708 - 1749)
      • Shiva Ji 2 and Tara Bai Managed to Escape to Kolhapur in Maharashtra
      💡 Marathas of Satara and Kolhapur were enemies

  • Phase 1 → Later Mughals, Advent & Companies Rule ( 1707 - 1858 )

    • The Later Mughals

      • Some Important Medieval Terms - From Class Handouts

        • Ranks

          • Wazir - Head of Revenue Administration - Finance Minister
            1. Central Finance Minister (Deewan-E-Wazirat)
            2. Wazir (Admin) = Revenue(Income) + Finance (Expenditure)
            3. Provincial Deewans - Statements - Income & Expenditure
            4. Imperial Treasure
          • Mir Bakshi - Head of Military Administration - Chief of Army Staff
            1. Gather Intelligence
            2. Military Recommendation - Appointment & Promotion
            3. Time to Time Salary (Paymaster General)
            4. Changes in Provincial Policies
          • Subadar, Sahib E Subah, Nazim - Head of Mughal Provincial Administration - Chief Minister
            1. Appointed by - Mughal Prince || Highest Mansab Officer
            2. Assisted By
              1. Sipahsalar - Rep of Emp - Civil + Military - Governor
              2. Diwan - Revenue
              3. Bakshi - Provinvial Army
              4. Sadr - Judicial
          • Zamindars - Financial Income + Social Prestige
            • Hereditary Land Holders - Descendants of Old Ruling Family
            • Types of Zamindars - According to Nurul Haran
              1. Primary - Proprietary Rights (मालिकाना हक़) - Propertied Subject of Emperor
              2. Secondary - Intermediary Rights + Help State in Collection of Land Revenue
              3. Autonomous Chiefs - Autonomous Rights - Had to Pay a Fix Amount as Peshakash to Emperor
                1. Peasants Cant be disposed if they pay land revenue
            • Characteristics - Inheritable, Saleable, Divisible
            • Obligation to State - Judicial & Policing Functions
          • Mansabdar & Jagirdar - Civil Services of Mughals
            • by Akbar in 1571 - for Administration
            • Who Holds a Mansab in Mughal Administration - Mughal Service - Civil & Military Post
            • Recruited by Emperor, Suggested by Mir Bakshi
            • Can be Transferred from Revenue to Military - Not Hereditary
            • Salary Commensurate to Rank (Zat & Sawar)
            • Mansabdar whose Payment = land = Jagirdar ( Given Jagir as Payment)
            • Jagirdar - Right to Collect Revenue from Land and Maintain Army
            • Jagir - Pay Salary of Staff + Salary of Mansabdar + Tribute to Emperor
            • In Aurangzeb Time, Jagirs Shrinked - Less Revenue - System Eventually Ended

        • Systems

          • Dahsala System & Zabti System - Standard System of Revenue Assessment - Todar Mals Bandobast
            1. By Raja Todar Mal - Finance Minister of Akbar - Intro in 1580 AD
            2. From - Lahore to Allahabad + Malwa & Gujarat
            3. Process -
              1. Average of Total Production for 10 Years for Each Crop
              2. Averaged with Average of All the Crops Above - Dahsala
              3. in Revenue Circle (Dastur) due to variation of Land
            4. Land Measurement System - Zabti
              1. Instead of Rope, Bamboo Attached with Iron Rings Used
          • Jizyah & Pilgrimage Tax
            1. Jizyah Collected by Islamic States from Non Muslim Subjects
            2. Adult Free Sane males Non Muslims - PAY
            3. Women Children Elder Handicapped Ill Insane Monks Hermit Slave and Non Muslim Foreigner - NOT PAY
            4. Exemption
              1. Who could not Afford to Pay
              2. Non Muslims Joining Military
            5. Pilgrim Tax - Non Muslims Visiting Pilgrimage, Visiting Temple Pay and Obtain Pass - Money Earned used in Administrative & Religious Expenses of Temple
          • Chauth & Sardeshmukhi By Marathas
            1. paid By : Unfriendly Muslim Rulers of Deccan = 1/4th Revenue = Non Plundering Tax - In Return they wouldn't Raid Muslim Territories
            2. Additional 10% Tax on Muslim Territories in Deccan, As Chhatrapti claimed to be Sardeshmukh hence Hereditary Tax

        • Muazzam/Bahadur Shah 1 / Shah Alam 1 (1707 1712) (BS) (Shah -i-Bekhabar)

          • Introduction
            1. Came to Power at the Age of 63
            2. Religiously Tolerant Ruler - No Destruction of Hindu Temples
            3. Redefined Mughal Relationships with other rulers in India
          • Rajput Policy
            • Two Rulers
              1. Jaisingh of Amber/Jaipur
              2. Ajit Singh of Marwar/Jodhpur
            • Over All
              1. Deployed Mughal Militaries in Jaipur and Jodhpur
              2. Resisted by Rulers and Subjects, Hence BS withdrew Military & Extended “Olive Branch”
              3. Rajput Rulers Demanded higher Mansab in Mughal Administration
                1. Jai Singh - Mansab of Malwa
                2. Ajit Singh - Mansab of Gujarat
              4. BS Denied the Mansabdari, Rajpur weren't Happy
          • Sikh Policy
            1. Last Sikh Guru - Guru Gobind Singh
            2. Relations Of Mughals and Sikh weren't good because
              1. Guru Arjan Dev - Executed by Jahangir in 1606
              2. Guru Tegh Bahadur - Executed by Aurangzeb in 1675
            3. BS asked Guru Gobind Singh to Come to South India for Meeting, Meanwhile BS was in South India to defeat his second brother
            4. Guru Gobind Sing was camping on the banks of River Godavari, Before the Meeting - Guru Gobind Singh was Assassinated in 1806 by Assassins of Wazir Khan
            5. Wazir Khan - Mughal Governor of Sirhind in Punjab Because Wazir Khans was Killed in the Past by Sikhs in war
            6. For Normal Sikhs it appeared that BS called Guru for Meeting and got him assassinated
            7. Under leadership of Banda Singh Bahadur - disciple of Guru Gobind Singh
              1. Revolt Started - Sikhs were a Formidable Military force
              2. Mughals couldn't defeat Sikhs alone
            8. Hence BS established friendship with
              1. Churaman Jat - Bharat Pur
              2. Chatarsal Bundela - Bundelkhand
            9. These New Friendships weren't easy and cheap they demanded Jagirs and Mansabs from ruler
              1. more the mansab given more loss of Revenue
            10. BS to consolidate hi position as ruler was involved in Reckless Grants of Jagirs - Hence was called Shah -i-Bekhabar
            11. BS was not able to defeat sikh during his time but was able to capture Lohgarh Fort in 1712
          • Maratha Policy
            1. Released Shahu from Prison and Shahu asked if he could collect Sardeshmukhi and Chauth
            2. BS instigated war between Shahu v/s Shivaji II (Tarabai)
            3. Shahu won and now BS allowed Shahu to collect Sardehshmukhi because Shahu was Sardesh of Sattara
            4. Granting Chauth would have made Maratha Powerful, Hence Denied
          • Shah i Bekhabar
            1. within a span of 5 year he spent almost 13 crores
            2. Died naturally in 1708

        • Shahu CH (1682-1749)

          1. was Mughal prisoner of Aurangzeb for 18 years between 1689 to 1707
          2. Battle of Khed 1707 with Shiva JI 2
          3. Became Chhatrapati 1708-1749
          4. Military Commandment of Shahu was Balaji Vishwnath
          5. Shahu made Balaji Vishwnath Peshwa and handed him all military and administrative rights
          6. Hence
            1. Peshwa - De Facto / Real Ruler
              1. Peshwa Ship was not hereditary, yet all Peshwas from time of Balaji Vishwnath were Descendants of Balaji Vishwnath, because of Merit and not by any rule
              2. Also Shahu Didn't had any son, hence in 1748 one year before his death Balaji Baji Rao made peshwa ship hereditary
            2. Chhattarpati - De Jure / Nominal Ruler

        • { Jahandar Shah (1712-1713) + Zulfiqar Khan } v/s { Saiyed Brothers + Farrukh Siyar }

          1. Generally Mughal Nobles supported most meritorious prince, But off late Mughal noble became power hungry
          2. they couldn't become rulers because they had no Mughal blood, but if they could install a puppet ruler they could Rule
          3. Hence Zufliqar Khan supported the weakest son of BS = Jahandar Shah (JS) to be a ruler
          4. JS hence appointed Zulfiqar Khan as the **Wazir (**Other Nobles Jealous and Similar Aspirations)
          5. Hence Mughal Nobles were involved in a Short Sighted Work
          6. Changes by Zulfiqar Khan
            • Rajput
              1. Friendship with Rajput
              2. Jizyah abolished in Rajput Land
              3. This made rajputs friends of Mughals especially Zulfiqar khan
            • Sikhs
              1. were still very unhappy because they thought there last sikh guru was killed by BS
            • Marathas
              1. were allowed Sardeshmukhi and Chauth indirectly
              2. indirectly meaning collection by Mughals governor of Deccan → hand over the collection to maratha
              3. if Maratha would go to collecting revenue Mughals prestige would be hurt
              4. Maratha Still Not happy
                1. Maratha royal family in prison
                2. they couldn't show off
            • Ijara / Revenue Farming by Zulfiqar Khan
              1. During time of BS Mughals economy had gotten weak and hence Zulfiqar tried renewing it - Hence Ijara introduced
              • Ijara
                • Process
                  1. Land Revenue Collection rights would be auctioned
                  2. Revenue Farmers were called Ijaradar
                  3. Ijaradar will give money to Peasants for farming
                  4. Peasants will pay back tax to Ijaradar
                  5. Ijaradar will keep his Profit & Pay to Mughal
                • Problem with Ijara Example
                  1. Earlier govt was collecting tax as 10,000
                  2. Now due to auction Mughals got 11,000
                  3. Now Ijaradars was expected to collect 11,000
                  4. but Ijaradars collected 13,000 from peasants or as much as he wanted peasants share decreased to 17,000
                  5. now other Ijaradars became jealous of each other
                  6. hence now new Ijaradars auctioned at 12,000 Mughals got 12,000
                  7. now he collected 14,000 from Peasants, Peasants Share decreased to 16,000
                  8. Ijaradars also got commissions from Mughals
                • Inferences from Ijaradari System
                  1. both govt and Ijaradars were becoming rich at the cost of peasants / Peasantry
                  2. Ijaradari and Zameendari System were in conflict but could be avoided if all zameendar became Ijaradars
                  3. Mughals emperors would had absolute right over land revenue collection system but
                    1. Peasants were always Landless primarily
                    2. also its not sure that land ownership was with the Emperor or with the Kingdom
          • Saiyed Brothers wanted to dethrone JS & install a new puppet ruler
            1. Abdullah Khan
            2. Hussain Ali Khan
          • Battle of Agra, 1712
            1. Jahandar Shah + Zulfikar Khan v/s Farrukh Siyar + Sayyid Brothers

        • Emergence of Powerful Noble Groups in Mughals IADH

          1. Iraani Nobles - Persia (Zulfikar Khan)
          2. Afghani Nobles - Afghanistan
          3. Durrani Nobles - Central Asia (Asaf Jahan)
          4. Hindustani Nobles - India

        • Balaji Viswanath (1713-1720)

          1. Era of Powerful Peshwa Started with BalaJi Vishwanath
          2. Capital
            1. Capital of Chhatrapati - Satara
            2. Capital of Peshwa - Pune
          3. He was a Brahmin from Konkan Coast - Military Commander, Became De Facto ruler of maratha
          4. Peshwaship was not hereditary
          • Deal - Sayyid Brother and Balaji Vishwnath
            1. Release of Maratha Royal Family
            2. Direct Chauth and Sardeshmukhi Rights of Deccan
            3. In Response he Promised 15,000 Cavalry Men

        • Farrukh Siyar+Sayyid Brother+Mir Quli Khan (1713 - 1719)

          1. Nephew of Jahandar Shah - Farrukh Siyar
          2. Sayyid Brothers Position - Tried Making Farrukh Siyar a Puppet Rulers
            1. Abdullah Khan - Wazir - Revenue Head
            2. Hussain Ali Khan - Mir Bakshi - Military Head
          3. Some of their works included
            1. Abolition of Jizyah and Pilgrimage Tax - All Over India
          4. Sikhs Defeated Decisively
            1. Banda Bahadur was defeated imprisoned and executed in 1716 in Gurdaspur
            2. With this Defeat Sikhs got divide into 12 Groups/Misls - Hence disunity in Sikhs
            3. only in 1790 one great sikh leader united all the Misls called Maharaj Ranjit Singh
          5. Royal Farman of 1717
          6. His deal with Sayyid Brother and Balaji Vishwnath, Sayyid Brothers then asked Farrukh Siyar to implement the deal but Farrukh Siyar denied.
          7. Hence Sayyid Brothers Imprisoned and Killed Farrukh Siyar, First Mughal Emperor to be killed by his Nobles
          8. Other Nobles tensed - Started conspiring Against Sayyid Brothers especially Mir Quli Khan or Nizam Ul Mulk Asaf Jahan
          9. Meanwhile Sayyid Brothers Ended up installing 4 Kings namely
            1. Farrukh Siyar - First Mughal Emperor to be Killed
            2. Rafi Ud Darajat - Shortest Rule 3 Months
            3. Rafi Ud Daulah
            4. Muhammad Shah Rangeela

        • Rafi ud Darajat (1719) - Shortest Rule

          1. Agreed to Deal of Sayyid Brothers and allowed royal farman to marathas
          2. died of lung disease probably Tubercluosis

        • Rafi Ud Daulah / Shah Jahan 2 (1719)

          1. Nothing important
          2. he was a opium addict - died due to dysentery

        • Muhammad Shah (1719-1748) / Rangeela / Roshan Akhtar- Sayyid +Nizam Ul Mulk+Invasion of Nadir

          • Introduction
            1. Name : Roshan Akhtar
            2. Colloquial Name : Rangeela
            3. Titular Name : Muhammad Shah
          1. Sayyid brothers tried to control Rangeela but he hated control so he started supporting Asaf Jahan
          • Death of Sayyid Brothers
            1. Abdullah Khan - Killed in Battle of Agra - 1722
            2. Hussain Ali Khan - Poisoned to Death - 1720

          1. Asaf Jahn was a Durrani and Faithful Mughal Noble and wanted to restore Mughal empire to its past glory

          2. Rangeela was highly influenced by Koki Jiu and Eunuch Hafiz Khan

          3. Asaf Jahan Resigned from Post of Wazir in 1724 and went to South India and Established the Independent state of Hyderabad for Himself in 1724

        • Causes of Decline of Mughal Empire

          • Nature of the Empire
            1. Vastness of Empire - Boundaries of Mughal Empire { Tip : Aurangzeb Boundary }
            2. Problem of Communication and Troop Mobilisation
            3. Decentralised Administration (DA) - Because of Vastness
              1. This kind of DA was good until strong rulers but with weak rulers this DA became in itself a disadvantage
          • Aurangzeb’s Policies
            1. Policy of Expansion
              1. for this he had to wage a Multitude of Wars
              2. Mughal State was constantly at War
              3. Unnecessary Expansion & Enmity - particularly towards Deccan Rulers
            2. Religious Orthodoxy
            3. Failure of Statesmanship
          • Political Causes
            1. Absence of a Fixed Rule of Succession - Fratricidal Wars (Brother Killing Brothers)
            2. Worthless, Weak Willed Luxury loving rulers
            3. Degeneration of Character & Organisation of Nobility - Short Sighted Pursuit of Power
            4. Rise of Regional Power - Maratha, Sikhs, Kingdom of Mysore, Independent State of Bengal and Awadh
            5. Accelerated by Foreign Invasions
              1. Irani - Nadir Shah
              2. Durrani - Ahmed Shah Abdali
            6. Under Estimation of Europeans - they further ventured into politics
          • Economic Causes
            1. Constant State of War of Mughals / Constant Warfare - Consumption of Men and Materials
              1. Internally
              2. Externally
            2. Worsening Condition of Peasants
            3. Stagnation of Industries and Trade (Specially Foreign)
              1. In 17th and 18th Century - India Contributed to 25% of Worlds Trade
              2. Here Science and Knowledge wasn't given due importance
              3. because of Industrial Revolution - Instead of Exporting Finished Goods we were Exporting Raw Materials
              4. Tsar Nicholas used to say that Commerce of the World is Nothing but Trade of India
            4. Economic Bankruptcy
              1. Loss of Resources due to Foreign Invasions
              2. Jagirdari Crisis
                1. 80% of revenue was sent to the Jagirdars
          • Socio Cultural Causes
            1. No Modern Spirit of Nationalism
              1. Whatever it existed was very narrow
              2. here Regionalism was Nationalism
            2. Promotion of Regional Interests
            3. Discriminatory Social Setup
              1. Varna System Later Turned into Caste System
              2. Irrespective of who ruled Rajput, Mughal, Maratha, Status of Lower Strata was always the same, hence they became Indifferent towards Political Issues and Setup
          • Military Causes
            1. Disloyal & Corrupt Mansabdars
              1. Not Maintaining Enough Army
            2. Soldiers were always on the Verge of Mutiny
              1. Due to Economic Crisis and No Rest
            3. Medieval Army
              1. Use of Medieval Weapons
            4. Absence of a Strong & Modern Navy
              1. Hence couldn't navigate through waters
              2. they chose to remain land based powers, originally they were landlocked central Asia
              3. they had vessels but they were trading ones not military ones
              4. Most European powers had Strong Navy
            5. Lack of S&T Development

      • Rise of Regional Power due to Decline of Mughal Empire

        • Succession States
          1. Ruled by Mughal Governors who started behaving as independent rulers. with decline in Central Mughal Authority
          2. Examples
            1. Bengal
              1. Established by Murshid Quli Khan
            2. Awad/Oudh
              1. Established in 1772 by Sadat Khan Burhan Ul Mulk
            3. Hyderabad
              1. Nizam Ul Mulk Asaf Jahan in 1724
            4. Carnatic
              1. Established in 1725 by Nawab Saadullah Khan
              2. Given the Proximity of Hyderabad and Carnatic, Nizam used to Control the Nawab
            5. Rajput States of Jaipur and Jodhpur
              1. Jai Singh - Jaipur
              2. Ajit SIngh - Jodhpur and Malwa
        • Rebellion States
          1. They were under enemies of Mughals, who tried to expand their territories at the cost of Mughal Territories
          2. Example
            1. Maratha Confederacy - Chhatrapati and Peshwas
            2. Punjab - 12 Sikh Misls (Groups)
            3. State of Farrukhabad
              1. Established in 1714, Mohammad Khan Bangash
              2. Called Bangash Pathans, meaning Afghan Origin
            4. Rohil Khand
              1. Established in 1721 by Ali Muhammad Khan
            5. Jat State of Bharatpur
              1. Modern Day Rajasthan
              2. Established in 1722 by Badan Singh
              3. Churaman Jat was from this State
              4. Suraj Mal was from this State
        • Autonomous States
          1. Autonomous States were at the remote corners or periphery of the Mughal Empire and were relatively outside the sphere of Mughal influence
          2. South
            1. Independent State of Mysores Ruled by Wodeyars
              1. Muslim Rulers made these Wodeyars as Puppets like Haider Ali (1761 to 1782) and Tipu Sultan (1782 to 1799) (De Facto Rulers)
            2. Multitude of Chief-tans or Polygars Ruling many places in Far South India
              1. Originally from Vijayanagar Empire became independent after disintegration of Vijayanagar Empire
              2. Example
                1. Kattaboman
                2. Vehu Nachiyar
          3. Western Coast
            1. Raja Martand Verma - Travancore
              1. Had a Powerful Navy
              2. Defeated Dutch Company in Battle of Kolachal 1741
          4. North Eastern India - Multitude of Tribal Kingdoms
        • Nature of these Mid 18th Century Independent Kingdoms and Rulers (All 3)

          1. They behaved as Independent Rulers but to legitimise their position they acknowledge Nominal Supremacy of Mughal Emperors over them

          2. Due to Decentralisation of Polity in India, Decentralisation Led to plethora of Regional Powers

          3. Essentially all disintegrated states were Secular in Nature

            1. Example : Haider and Tipu Sultan Muslim, Their PM was a Hindu

          4. According to Western Historians - Decline of Mughal Empire and Rise of Regional Power led to Lawlessness but it was more of a smooth transition than anarchy

          5. Aped Mughal Administrative Structure - Regional Powers had mindlessly copied Mughal Administration with some regional influence and No Innovation

          6. Medieval Nature of the Army and No Powerful Navy - Primarily because almost all regional powers were landlocked states

            1. Exceptions : Raja Martand Verma and Tipu Sultan

          7. Rent Extracting States - Backward Looking Economic Structures

            1. Land Revenue + Custom Duties + Tools - Dependent on Land Revenue
            2. No Debt Market, No Central Banks, No Stock Market - No Copy of Europe

          8. Decline of Agriculture - Stagnation of Industries

            1. Due to Modern Scientific Agricultural Practise
            2. Due to Constant State of Warfare
            3. Due to Heavy Dependency of Indian Agriculture on Monsoon
            4. Food Security Threatened
            5. No Modern Industries

          9. Declining Foreign Trade

          10. Superstitious Ideas and Social Structure Based on Inequalities

            1. Caste System and Associated Inequalities
            2. Indifferent Towards Politics
            3. Why Superstitious ?
              1. Easy to Manipulate
            💡 Sugars White Colour From : Filtered through Bones - Gelatin Cow : Milk is Extracted from Cows Blood India : Launch Rockets with Nariyal In Military : A Day to Worship Guns Caste System : Extremely Superstitious Rajasthan : Child Marriage Eating Beef : is a Taboo in India but India is Largest Exporter of Beef in India

          11. Lacked Modern Nationalistic Ideas

            1. Narrow idea of Regionalism and Nationalism
            2. Internal Rivalry - King A reaching to Europeans to defeat King B
              1. Gradual dependence on Europeans
              2. Opportunity to Europeans to Enter India
    • The Advent of Europeans in India

      • Introduction

        Greeks and Romans came to India they were Europeans

      • Historical Trade in India - Arabs & Europeans

        1. Middlemen in Trade Between India and West
          1. Arabs
          2. Italian
            1. Geneva
            2. Venecia
        2. Around 7th Century AD Islams was born in the Deserts of Arabia
          1. As Islam Started Spreading
          2. Muslims came in conflict with Christians - Crusades 11th and 13th Centuries - Old Trade Routes Closed
          3. As a Result, Constantinople, Turks Belonging to Ottoman Empire captured Istanbul
          4. Here If Europeans could Establish a New Trade Route, they could sell to East but there would be many countries in between ( Tax and Transport of Goods would be High)
          5. Particularly Portugal, Spain, Britain and France were jealous of Italians making money
          6. Hence they tried finding out a Direct Sea Route between West and East, they would have saved lots of Tax and Tolls
          7. Rulers of West supported such Explorations ( Supported Scientist, Geographers and Companies )
            1. This would help in Spread of Christianity
            2. This would help their economic Prosperity by Foreign Trade & Tax

      • Hence Quest in Western European Countries to find new trade routes

        • Countries (EDPFN)
          1. England
          2. Denmark
          3. Purtugal
          4. France
          5. Netherlands - Holland
        • Primary Reasons
          1. Islamic Wars for Territorial War
          2. Old Trade Routes - Closed in 7th Century AD
          3. Very High cost of Transmitting Goods
          4. The capture of Constantinople (Istanbul) by Turks in 1453
        • Encourage by Factors such as
          1. Emergence of Nationalistic Sentiments in Europeans Emergence of Nation State → Strong Rulers wanting National Glory
          2. Wish For Progress of Trade and Prosperity of Economics
          3. Possibility of Spreading Christianity
          4. S&T Development due to Renaissance

      • Three Terms forming Basis of Modern Europe - Merc, Imperial, Colonial

        1. Mercantilism

          Mercantilism is an economic policy that is designed to maximise the exports and minimise the imports for an economy. It promotes imperialism, colonialism, tariffs and subsidies on traded goods to achieve that goal

        2. Imperialism

          a policy of extending a country's power and influence through colonisation, use of military force, or other means

        3. Colonialism

          the policy or practice of acquiring full or partial political control over another country, occupying it with settlers, and exploiting it economically

      • Europeans Reaching in India (Order in Which Europeans came to India) PDuB DaF

        • Portuguese - Portugal
          • Vasco Da Gama 20 may 1498 in Koppad near Calicut, Most Important Discovery during age of discovery
            1. Rulers of Calicut were Zamorins Dynasty - Ruler Name Samuthiri
            2. No successful Commercial Treaty but a Handsome Fortune
              1. Was allowed to sale his goods and purchase Spices from Kerala Coast
            3. When Vasco sold spices in Europe he earned 6000 percent profit
          • Forced Trade Methods of Europeans
            1. Using Modern Weapons and Modern Navy - were threatening Indian Traders to sell them cheap
            2. Portuguese used Force to Trade
            3. This Strained Relations with Zamorin - Frequent Fights between Zamorin and Portuguese
          • Casa Da India a Govt Company (CDI)
            1. They Sent Pedro Alvares Cabral 1500 AD
            2. He started constructing Factories in Calicut, Cochin and Cannanore (Kannur, Kerala) along Malabar Cost
            3. Factory Meaning Set of Buildings - Including
              1. Living Quarters of Portuguese and Some Storage facilities
            4. Portuguese Factories were UnSafe
              1. Indian Traders of Gujarat Primarily Muslims Traders
              2. Arabian Traders Unhappy because they were bypassed
          1. So, Portuguese Appointed Governor for Portuguese Factory in India called Francisco De Almeida - First Governor and Viceroy of the Portuguese India
          2. Factories and Places Near Portuguese Factories were called - EPI
          3. Hence Summary of European Heads is
            1. Factory - Headed by a Factor
            2. Fort - Headed by a Governor
          4. Later EPI were used synonymous with CDI - Technically Not Correct
          • During times of Almeida Factories became Forts
            1. First Portuguese/European Fort in India - Fort Emmanuel / Fort Manuel 1503 AD
            2. There is No Difference between Factory and Fort
            3. Factory + High Protective Wall = Fort
          • Another Man who contributed to the construction of Fort was Alfonso De Albuquerque
          • Confederacy of Egypt, Turkey and Gujarat - Attacked Portuguese Possessions in India
            1. Portuguese defeated their Enemies
            2. They realised Land Expansion wouldn't be Feasible as it would meet with Resistance
          • Hence Francisco de Almeida Started Blue Water Policy
            1. Wanted to Control Indian Ocean ( Trade and Commerce ) because of their Modern Navy - From Gulf of Aden to Sp Islands of Indonesia
            2. Use of Powerful Portuguese Navy
            3. Establishing Portuguese Coast
          • Hence they Installed Cartaz System
            1. Naval License Issued by CDI at Low Cost
            2. Ship having Cartaz License - they could use the facilities and provide naval help
            3. Ships Under Cartaz System - Could Only Dock under Portuguese Ports
              1. In Goa, Malacca and Ormuz
            4. CS - Eliminated Competition & Import Export became Easy
            5. Due to Cartaz System Portuguese were able to monopolise trade in India Ocean
              1. Monopoly of Spice Trade and Other Products
          • Despited all this Almeida wasn't considered Founder of Portuguese in India
          • Alfonso De Albuquerque (1509-1511) is founder of Portuguese in India
            • Vijayanagar Rulers and Portuguese were Friends because
              1. Vijayanagar Krishnadevraay was Enemy with Bahmani Kingdom
              2. Bahamani had Awesome Cavalry
              3. Vijayanagar needed Horses
              4. Portuguese helped Vijayanagar Empire to Import Horses
            1. Fort Manuel in Cochin in 1503 AD
            2. Battle of Goa, 1510 AD
              1. Albuquerque v/s Ismail Adil Shahi
              2. later Goa became Portuguese capital in India
            3. Established Mint for Portuguese, 1510 AD
            4. Banned Practise of Sati, 1511 AD in Goa
            5. Numerical Advantage
              1. Encouraged Christanity
                1. Fair - Awareness
                2. Fowl - Torture
              2. Encouraged Portuguese Sailors to marry Indian Women
              3. Good relations with Vijayanagar.
            6. Hence called Founder of Portuguese Empire in India
          • Important Points of Portuguese
            1. Intolerant and Fanatical in religious matters - forcible conversions
            2. Use of force with trade - Gunboat Policy - Control over high seas - Cartaz System - Naval Vessels of Portuguese was called Floating Fort
            3. Nuno Da Kunha (1528-1538) - Captured Daman and Diu

        • Prominent Features of Europe

          • Middle Age & Crusades { 500-1500+1095-1270 }

            1. Middle Age - { 500-1500 AD } - Period of European Countries Interpreting it & Fall of Western Roman Empire
            2. Crusades - Series of Military Campaigns by Pope & Christian Western Powers to take Jerusalem from Muslims & Defend it.
            3. 8 Major Official Crusades - (1095 -1270) + Many Un Official Ones

          • Commercial Revolution - Set Stage for Industrial Revolution

            1. From 1000-1300 → Europe Population*2→Urbanisation→Commercial Revolution = Sophisticated Monetisation System
            2. CR Stimulus - Voyages + Surge in Overseas Trade + Mercantilism Accepted
            3. Economic Stimulus - Creation of Money Economy + Economic Specialisation + Estab of Bank, Future Market, Bourse

          • Renaissance - French for Rebirth

            1. Interest in Classical Scholarship & Values, Decline of Feudal System
            2. New Continents Found, Paper, Printing, GunPowder, Mariner Compass
            3. Copernican replaced Ptolemaic Astronomy

          • Age of Discovery / Exploration { 1400-1600 }

            1. Global Exploration Took Place in Europe and around the world

          • Agriculture Revolution - 17th to 19th Century

            1. Reallocation of Land Ownership - Make Land Compact
            2. Inventions in Technology - M/C, Drainage System etc
            3. Sceintific Methodology of Breeding
            4. Experimentation with New Crop + Sys of Crop Rotation

          • Sceintific Revolution / Sceintific Renaissance

            1. Started in Europe → Towards End of Renaissance → Influenced Enlightenment

            Beginning of SR

            1. Nicolas Copernicus, 1543 - Book - On Revolution of Heavenly Spheres

            Ending of SR

            1. Galileo Galiee, 1632 - Dialogue - Two Chief Worlds

            Completion of SR

            1. Isaac Newton, 1687 - Principia - Laws of Motion, Universal Gravitation = System of New Cosmology

          • Industrial Revolution, Britain, 18th Century

            1. Transformation From Agrarian to Handicraft Economy → Dominated by Industry + m/c Manufacturing
            2. There were Technical, Socio Political & Cultural Changes
            3. Changes
              1. New Basic Material - Iron and Steel
              2. New Energy Source - Coal, Steam Engine, ICE, Fuel, Motive Power
              3. New Machines - Spinning Jenny & Power Loom
              4. New Organization of Work - Factory, Inc DOL, Specialisation of Skills
              5. New Tran & Comm - Automobile, Airship, Telegram and Radio
              6. New Application of Science to Industry - Use of Nat Resources + Mass Production of Manufactured Goods

          • Age of Reasons / Enlightenment - Late 17th to End of Napoleonic Wars in 1815

            1. Rigorous Scientific Political and Philosophical Discourse
            2. Decisive in Making of Modernity
            3. Centuries of Customs and Traditions - Thrown Away in Favour of ( Exploration, Individualism, Tolerance & Scientific Temper)
            4. Dev ( Politics + Industry ) = Modern World

      • Companies Rule - Part 2 Dual Govt, Foundation, First Anglo-Mysore

        • Dual Government in India (1765 - 1772)
          1. This complex setup was called Dual Govt
            1. Puppet Nawab - Had All Responsibilities but No Power
            2. BEIC - Enjoyed all Powers but had No Responsibilities
          2. Its always about two power centres, controlling the same entity as in Bengal - Diarchy/Dyarchy
          3. Classic Example of Diarchy - Indian Marriage Setup
          4. Hence Bengal was Exploited Rampantly to the Extent of 6 Million Pounds of that time from Bengal
          5. Rampant Exploitation + Drought = Led to Bengal Famine of 1770 = 1/3 of Bengal Population Died i.e 10 Million Dead
          6. Warren Hastings in 1772 - Ended Dual Govt - because he was confident that BEIC could operate on its own now
          7. All Apparatus of Munger was now shifted to Calcutta - Calcutta became Administrative HQ of Warren Hastings
        • Foundation of British Rule in India (1767-1773)
          1. Company had morphed into a Semi Sovereign Political Entity in India
          2. George 3 couldn't ignore humanitarian crisis in India, hence wanted to control British affairs in India
          3. British Apprehensions
            1. Other British Capitalists were Jealous
            2. Politicians were alarmed as → BEIC could install Dual Government in Britain because of money at their disposal
            3. Britishers returning from India were ridiculed as Nabobs
          4. In 1767 BEIC was asked to pay 4 Lac Pounds Per Annum, to lower britishers tax. Company Paid.
          5. In 1771 BEIC Applied for a Loan of 1 Million Pound, Parliament got suspicious
          6. Secret Parliamentary committee appointed founded corruption in Company, Since Company was on the verge of Bankruptcy, Crown used this opportunity to control companies affairs in India.
          7. Regulating Act of 1773, to regulate activities of company in India.
          8. With 1857 Revolt Crown abolished Companies Rule and established Crowns Rule

                  2. Appellate - British Sub in Presidency of Madras and Bombay by Appeal

                  3. Hence case of unclear Jurisdiction
                  4. Hence GGIC wanted Individual Jurisdiction

              • Amending Act of 1781 / Declaratory Act of 1781
                • to solve issue of jurisdiction between GGIC and SC
                • Supreme Court lost its Appellate Jurisdiction, SC Jurisdiction was restricted to Fort Williamn and Calcutta, Appeals from Other Provinces went to Sadr Court
                • SC lost jurisdiction over employees and officer of company hence they were now trailed by GGIC
                • Hence Sadr Court Became Superior
                • Promised that Indian Customs and Religious importance would be given importance
                • First Act to Separate Executive from Judiciary
              • Pitts India Act, 1784
                • British Parliament Created a New Body Called BOC ( Board of Control 6 Members )
                • BOC 6 - In England
                  • 4 By Crown
                    • From Privy Council to Advisory Council
                  • 2 by British Parliament
                    • Chancellor of Exchequer - Finance Minister
                    • Secretary of State - Minister of Parliament
                  • Head of BOC = President of BOC = Sydney
                  • Exclusive Control - Civil Military & Revenue Affairs
                • COD 24 - In England
                  • Commercial Affairs
                • Created Dual Control over Indian British Administration
                • Administrative Deadlock Resolution
                  • GG of Bengal was Still Not Solved
                  • Hence Strength of GG Council was Reduced to 3 for smooth administration
                  • Casting Vote - GG of Bengal could exercise In case of Tie
                • as COD and BOC were in Britain - hence Indian Territories by Britishers were British Possessions
                • GG of Bengal = GG of Bombay + GG of Madras
                • GG of Bengal - Clear Sub Ordination in Matters of War, Diplomacy and Revenue
                • General Ban on Aggressive Wars and Treaties
                • A Link Between GG and BOC out of this 24 COD - 3 were chosen as “Secret Committee of Directors” - Appointed by British Parliament

                • And hence Annual Auctions were Introduced
                • Because of Exploitation of Peasants - Agrarian Crisis Introduced

                • Most British Rulers in India were Orientalist

            • Administrative Development

              • Act of 1786

                1. Cornwallis was allowed to exercise power of Commander in Chief
                2. Veto under Special Circumstances
                3. GGB became effective Ruler

              • Cornwallis Code, 1793

                • Civil Services Reforms
                  1. Merit was Introduced for Recruitment of Civil Services
                  2. No Recommendation was accepted in appointment of Civil Services
                  3. All High Positions in Civil Services was made exclusive for Europeans
                  4. Indians were restricted to Lower Ranks in Civil Services - He thought Indians were Disloyal, In Efficient & Non Meritorious
                  5. Recruitment of Civil Servants was done by COD and sent them to India. COD had complete monopoly over recruitment of civil servants from (1793-1853/54)
                  6. BOC was Historically Accused of Nepotism ; Cornwallis was against this.
                  7. Prospective Civil Servants had to Sign an Agreement or a Covenant and Agree that
                    1. They Will Not Accept Bribe
                    2. They Will Not be involved in Private Trade
                    3. Disclose their assets before entering into Civil Services
                  8. Signing this and Entering Civiil Services - Covenanted Civil Servant
                  9. Cornwallis’s Civil Services under company was made Highest Paying Job under the World
                  10. Father of Indian Civil Services - Corn Wallis
                  11. He Felt that too many powers were concentrated in Hands of District Collector - Judiciary and Administrative
                    1. Hence Cornwallis Separated Revenue Administration from Civil Jurisdiction
                • Police Reforms
                  1. Historically Police function was done by Mughals. Cornwallis introduced Europeanised form of Police
                  2. Province → District(SP) → Circle(Inspector) → Tanas
                  3. SP → Europeans && Inspectors (Darogas)→ Indians
                  4. Father of Indian Police - Cornwallis
                  5. They were given unlimited power and hence they had become Oppression Arm of BEIC
                • Judiciary Reforms
                  1. Introduced 4 Tier Judiciary
                  2. Cornwallis abolished Jurisdiction between Civil and Revenue Cases
                  3. Revenue Courts also Called ( Maal Adalats)
                  4. 4 Tier
                    1. Local
                    2. District
                    3. Provincial
                    4. Apex Court + Supreme Court (Dummy Court)
                  5. Indian Judges - Were Restricted to the Local Court, Specially Civil Judiciary
                  6. Indian Judges in Criminal Judiciary worked under Europeans
                  7. Appeals from Sadr Court - To Privy Council of British Monarch

                  • Summary1

                    /Screenshot_2022-08-30_at_10.28.30_AM.png)

                  • Summary2

                    /Screenshot_2022-08-30_at_10.31.20_AM.png)

                  1. Positive Impacts of Judiciary Reforms
                    1. Inhumane Punishments were Abolished, Civilised Punishment like - Hang Till Death was Introduced
                    2. Indians could Sue Indian Officers of the Company
                  2. Negative Impact of Judiciary Reforms
                    1. Judiciary, Particularly Provincial courts had become inefficient due to many levels
                    2. because they didn't meet regularly, they met only twice a year, burden on administration. Hence Pendency of Cases Increased.
                    3. Justice had become Costly & Time Consuming
                    4. Police Violence on Under Trials Increased, as they didn't meet regular, police custody violence increased

                CORNWALLIS EUROPEANISED INDIAN ADMINISTRATION, JUDICIARY & POLICE

                BOC was Initially paid until 1793 from British Parliament, then from BEIC Treasury

            • Socio Economic & Cultural Developments
              1. Establishment of Sanskrit College in Banaras, 1791 - By Jonathan Duncan
                1. To Promote Development of Sanskrit Literature
                2. All Britishers were Orientalist
                3. Hidden Agenda - Brits wanted to Master Hindu Law
              2. Prelude to Permanent Settlement - Option to Revenue Farming - Needed for Stabilised Income
                1. Procure Raw Material Advance
                2. Salaried of Bureaucrats
                3. Conquest and Wars be Planned
              3. Cornwallis Introduced - Zameendari or Permanent Settlement, 1793
                1. By John Shore & James Grant
                2. Aim
                  1. Maximum, Stable Income
                  2. Political Allies
                  3. Increase Agricultural Production in India
                3. Process
                  1. 45-50% of Revenue for 10 Years
                4. Introduced in
                  1. Bengal, Bihar, Orissa, Parts of Madras & Carnatic
              4. Land Owning System
                1. They Didn't own the land but because they were paying revenue regularly they had some hereditary rights on the land and hence weren't evicted form the Land
                2. Britishers felt that if some Indians could be made the owners of Land, so that they may take care of land and increase efficient and output of Production
                3. Cornwallis Identified some people and declared them owners of this Land
                4. Until Zameendar's & His Descendants paid Revenue Regularly they could be owners of Land
              5. Method of Calculation
                1. Land Revenue Calculated in terms of Bags or Money
                2. Ex : Land Produce = 220 Bags of Rice
                3. Hence Land Revenue would be : 110 Bags of Rice
                4. Zameendar had to Collect this 110 and submit it to the government
                  1. Govt - 10/11 - 100 Bags
                  2. Z’Dar - 1/11 - 10 Bags
                5. Remaining 110 Bags - Shared Between Z’Dar and Peasants
                6. In Case of Bumper Produce - Zameendar would get Surplus - Zameendar became Rich - Zameendar became a Political Friend of British - Zameendar would control revolt now not Britishers
                7. Zameendar's would Breakwater of Storms - Commentary
                8. Zameen’s Patta would be in the Name of Zameendar’s
                9. Peasants were not only Exploited but also lost Hereditary Rights of their Land
              6. Why Advantageous to Britishers
                1. Made Political Allies
                2. Increased Revenue
                3. Gave Stabilised Income
              7. Negative Impact of Zameendari System
                1. Exploitation of Peasants
                2. Govt was illusive, Zameendar made more Money than Govt
            • Miscellaneous Development under Cornwallis

          • Richard Wellesley, 1798-1805

            • Introduction of Subsidiary Alliance, 1798
              1. Major Diplomatic Military Policy
              2. Duplex used French Army as a Mercenary Force → inspired Clive → Policy of Ring of Fence → W Hastings Promoted → Gradual Perfection by British Rulers → Subsidiary Alliance of Wellesley, 1798, Nizam of Hyderabad First to accept it

              • Subsidiary Alliance had 4 Stages

                STAGE 1 - Friendship & Buffer State

                1. British Company Approached Indian Rulers and Offered Friendship as well as Military Assistance in case of an Emergency

                STAGE 2 - Military Assistance

                1. Company Provided Military Assistance to the Friendly Rulers & Helped them achieve victories in Local Battles, Wars and Conflicts
                2. Hence, Company Demonstrated their Military Superiority

                STAGE 3 - Subsidiary Treaty

                1. Company convinced Indian Rulers to Disband his Native Military in place of which Company was to { RECRUIT, TRAIN & COMMAND} a SUBSIDIARY ARMY
                2. This Subsidiary Army was to be Maintained at the cost of Indian Subsidiary Rulers
                3. The Money Ruler Paid to the Company was Called - Subsidiary Money
                4. State or Country - Subsidiary State
                5. Subsidiary Army was to provide protection to Subsidiary Ruler against both Internal & External Threats
                6. but Army remained loyal to British Commanders and not to the Subsidiary Rulers
                7. Gradually Subsidiary Ruler became dependent upon Subsidiary Army and Company Insisted that he shouldn't control his Foreign Policy - { Friends & Foe was to be decided by Company}
                8. Company also Installed their Political Agent { RESIDENT } at the Capital of Subsidiary State & his purpose was to cause political chaos leading to Annexation of Subsidiary State ultimately

                STAGE 4 - Subjugation

                1. Company Increased Subsidiary Money Exorbitantly that the Indian Ruler couldn't afford to pay in lieu of Pending Dues he was convinced to CEDE or GIVE AWAY parts of his territories with complete SOVEREIGN RIGHTS to the Company
                2. Gradually, The Subsidiary Ruler was reduced to the Position of Complete Dependence and his State has been rendered IMPOTENT.

              • The Primary Goals of Subsidiary Alliance were

                1. To Make Company Supreme Rulers of Indian Territories
                2. To Maintain a Large Standing Army at the Cost of Indian Rulers
                3. To Not Let Indian Rulers Unite Against the Company as they controlled the foreign policy
                4. To Keep the French out of Indian Politics given “Napoleonic Danger and Threat to British”

              • Effects of Subsidiary Alliance, 1798

                1. Thus Wellesley used Subsidiary Alliance as Trojan Horse & a Double Edged Sword to expand British territories in India and to keep the French at Bay at the Same time
                2. Hence No attempt of Grand Alliance is Seen in the 19th Century.

              • Primary Features

                1. Dissolve Army & Pay for it. If Not able to, Give Away Portions of Land
                2. B Non Interference in Internal Affairs
                3. Cannot Enter into Alliance with other Foreign Power, No Foreign National, Surrender Foreign Affairs to B → Curb Napoleonic Danger
                4. Indian = Lost Powers in Military & Foreign = Lost Power = British “Protectorate” → Full time British Resident in Indian Court
            • Fourth Mysore War, 1799
              1. Arthur Wellesley Military Commander and Brother of Richar was killed in Battle of Seringapatnam

            • Second Maratha War, 1803-1806

              • Yashwant in 1802 attacked Peshwa & Scindia and Defeated them in Battle of Poona or Battle of Hadaspar
              • Peshwa Escaped from Poona, Yashwant Rao Installed Relative Vinayak Rao as new Peshwa

              • Marathas couldn't digest a Subsidiary Peshwa hence they tried to Defeat British Individually and got defeated except for Gaikwads ( who had already signed Subsidiary Treaty)
              • Bhonsle - Treaty of Devgaon, 1803 (Subsidiary Treaty)

          • John Adams, 1823

            • Socio Economic Cultural Development
              • General Committee of Public Instructions
                1. Committee Consisted of Ten Members like H T Princep, Thomas Babbington Macaulay, H H Elison and most of the member were admirers of Classical or Oriental Learning
                2. Actions of Orientalism
                  1. It Set Up Sanskrit College in Calcutta, Agra, Delhi
                  2. Introduced English Classes in all oriental colleges;
                  3. undertook the printing & publications of Sanskrit and Arabic Books at a large scale
                  4. Employed Scholars to Translate English Books in to the Oriental Languages
                3. GCPI was clearly inclined towards Orientalism but public opinion was rapidly growing gin favour of English Education due to several reasons
                4. The Missionaries had greatly popularised English Education
                5. Indian Leader like Raja Ram Mohan Roy urged Indians to Study the English Languages and to acquire a knowledge of the western science
                6. Study of English was Essential to obtain Lucrative posts under govt as it was the Language of the rulers
                7. English Education was regarded a panacea for all social ills
              • Press Regulations, 1823
                1. Wanted to Regulate Press Activities in India.
                2. He Passed Press Regulations for Press
                3. Extinguished the Freedom of Unlicensed Printing - Operating a Press without a License a Punishable Offence
                4. Every Publisher was required by the Government to Obtain a License
                5. The Government also had the Authority to revoke the License
                6. Default to Pay a Penalty of Rs 400 and the Government would shut Down the Press
                7. Buckhingham’s Calcutta Journal and Raja Ram Mohan Roy’s Mirat Ul Akhbar had to Cease Publication

                1. Conflict b/w Brits & Russia
                2. Ended at time of Viceroy Elgin II
                3. The Great Game between England and Russia Began in 1830 and Lasted Throughout the 19th Century
                4. Rivalry Between Britain & Russia to Protect their Spheres of Influence in Central Asia
                5. England used Afghanistan as a Buffer State to Protect all approaches to British India by Russia
                6. British Concerns about the Russian Influence on Afghanistan led to the Anglo Afghan War
                7. Two Anglo Afghan War - 2

              • Mahalwari Settlement, 1833

                • Introduction by Bentick

                • Designing by - Holt Mackenzie & Robert Merrtings Bird

                • Objective

                  1. Maximise Income
                  2. Collective Ownership of Lands
                  3. Collective Responsibility of Tax Payment

                • Details

                  1. 66% for 25-30 Years
                  2. Introduced in NWFP, Punjab, Gangetic Plains and Central India
                  3. Permanent + Ryotwari = Mahalwari

                • Process

                  • Basic Unit of Revenue Collection = Mahal = Village or a Group of Village

                  • Collectively Own & Collectively Pay

                  • If one person couldn't pay the share of his Land Revenue - Peasants Helped by Peasants or Peasants would buy it

                  • Collected by Head of Village - Lambardar

                    • Not a Owner of Land but a Middle Man - type of Permanent
                    • Land was Owned by Peasants - type of Ryotwari

                  • Schema

                    /Screenshot_2022-09-01_at_11.47.10_AM.png)

                  • They would pay Land Revenue Share as well if they owned land as well

                • Lambardars could be fired hence they were angry

                • Surplus was already Added to the Pre Fixed Amount in the Land Revenue Decided

                  1. Cultural Sub-Jugation

                    1. Proficient in English

                    3. Masters Don’t Learn Language of Slaves but other wise
                  3. If Indians could be Westernised - Better Market for Britishers

                • Downward Filtration Theory of Education

                  • Britishers Didn't want to Spend Money
                  • Suggested Only Educating Only Top Class People, So Lower Class could Follow
                  • Macaualy States that Giving Education to the Members of the Higher Class of the Society would Facilitate an Opportunity for the Grass Root People of Society, too, because the lower class people alway tend to Imitate and follow the Model of the people of higher status in the society

          • Dalhousie, 1848-1856 - VVIMP
            1. Actual Name : James Andrew Ramsay
            • Political Events

              • Doctrine of Lapse

                1. The British Rulers before Dalhousie generally avoided Annexing friendly Indian States (Subsidiary States) unless it was absolutely necessary.
                2. But Dalhousie (under influence of COD) made it a Policy to annexe Indian Subsidiary States using Doctrine of Lapse, citing various other REASONS
                3. According to “Doctrine of Lapse”, an Adopted Son of an Indian Subsidiary Ruler could inherit the Personal Property of his Foster Father but couldn't claim the right to succeed to his Throne
                4. it was for the Paramount Power (British) to decide whether to bestow Kinship on Adopted Son or Not ?
                5. Dalhousie wanted to Finish Quest of M Hastings - What Hastings Initiated (Paramountcy Policy) Dalhousie made Britishers Paramount

                • Annexations

                  1. Annexation of Satara, Jaitpur, Sambalpur, Udaipur, Jhansi, Nagpur & Awadh (For MisGovernance)

                • Example of Jhansi

                  1. King : Ganga Dhar Rao
                  2. Queen : Laxmi Bai
                  3. Son : Damodar - Died as Infant
                  4. Adopted a Son
                    1. Original Name ; Anant Rao
                    2. ReNamed as : Damodar Rao
                  5. King Health Failed, on Deathbed by 1853 ; before he dies agents of company promised that his adopted son would succeed to throne. Believing this he died peacefully
                  6. After his Death ; Dalhousie Annexed Jhansi in 1853 ; Hence Rani Laxmi Bai Participated in Revolt of 1857

                • Example of Awadh

                  1. Nawab : Wajid Ali Shah
                  2. Wife : Hazrat Mahal
                  3. Son : Brijis Qadir
                  4. BKG : Awadh was friendly State of Britisher from 1765
                  5. Nawabs, Soldier → Proud of Friendship of British
                  6. In Fact - 1/3 Soldier of Britishers = from Awadh
                  7. Dalhousie felt Wajid Ali Shah - Was Mismanaging Economic Affairs
                  8. in 1856 he announced Annexation of Awadh on Economic Crisis - He Didn't even Tried Brijis Qadir
                  9. Annexation was a Rude Shock to Ruling Family, Nobles, Soldiers of Awadh in British Army Resentful ;
                  10. Hazrat Mahal participated in 1857

                • Pension of Nana Saheb - Adopted Son of Nana Saheb Stopped

                  Hence he Participated in 1857 too

              • British Economic Policy wrt India

                • Period of Mercantilism, (Mid 18th Century to 1813)
                  1. Direct Loot & Plunder
                • Period of Free Trade / Lassies Faire (1813 - Mid 19th Century)
                  1. Promoting one way free trade between India and England
                  2. Export of Indian Finished Goods to England - DISCOURAGED
                  3. Import of British Industrial Textile to India ENCOURAGED ( All Most Tax Free)
                  4. India Exported Raw Materials
                  5. India had Negative Balance of Trade
                  6. India Became Economic Colony
                • Period of Finance Imperialism / Scientific Exploitation (Mid 19th Century - Independence)
                  1. Due to First IR, British Capitalist had Loads of Money
                  2. British Capitalist were encouraged to invest this money in India for which Govt Promised Guaranteed Returns
                  3. Hence British Capital Started Dominating Indian Economy
                  4. One Expression of Financial Imperialism - Indian Railways

              • Woods Dispatch, 1854

                • President of Board of Control

                • He Sent a Letter to Dalhousie - Called Woods Dispatch

                • Charles Woods Letter is Called Magna Carta of Indian Education System - He told “Downward Filtration Theory had Failed” - Govt Should Take Action

                • He Suggested

                  1. An Educational Department was to be Set in Every Province
                  2. Universities on the Model of London Universities be established in Big cities such as Bombay, Calcutta and Madras
                  3. At Least One Govt School be Opened in Every District
                  4. Affiliated Private Schools should be Given Grand in Aid
                  5. The Indian Natives should be given training in their mother tongue too
                  6. Division of Medium of Languages
                    1. Primary Education - Vernacular
                    2. Secondary Education - Anglo Vernacular
                    3. Higher Education - Englsih
