  • Mid Phase → Reaction - Resistance - Result (1857)

    • Reaction - Subtle - Socio Religious Reform Movements (SRRM)

      • By Products of British Rule in India

        1. Spread of Modern Education & Increasing Awareness - we started comparing ourselves with other world
        2. Reaction Against Spread of Christianity
        3. Rediscovery of Indian Past - Association with India Proud Identity - Which Meant Changing Religious Status Quo of Present
        4. Rising Tide of Nationalism also contributed to Socio Religious Forms

      • SRRM Condition at times of British

        All Social Evils had origins in Caste System

        • Backward Looking Societal Setup - Caste System & Inequalities - You Can Change Nationality but not Cast

        • Inhuman Practises in Indian Society

          1. Female Infanticide
          2. Purdah System
          3. Dowry
          4. Child Marriage
          5. Polygamy
          6. Sati
          7. Plight og Widows
          8. Inequalities against women - Education Employment, inheritance, marriage & divorce

        • Superstitious Beliefs & Complex Rituals - Exploitation of the masses by upper caste people

          • Lower caste was easy to Manipulate - Cuz Illiterate - At time of Independence Literacy Rate was ___

        • Illiteracy & lack of Modern Education - Woods Dispatch - Downward Filtration Theory had Failed

        • Britishers Heavily criticised Indian System - To justify their rule in India

        • Some Woke Indians - Started Reacting Against Britishers

        • Socio Religious - Change the Socity by Changing religion because inherent evil was practised from religion

      • SRRM Factors Responsible

        1. Presence of Colonial Govt in India
        2. Condition of Indian Society
        3. Increasing Western Criticism - White Mans Burden served as Justification for British Rule in India - Racial Arrogance
        4. Consciousness of Defeat by Foreign Power
          1. It was more hurtful because it was specifically by rulers

      • SRRM Some Facts

        Reformist (Reason & Conscience) v/s Revivalist Movements (Tradition)

        REFORMIST - BRAHMO, PRARTHANA, ALIGARH - FORWARD MOVEMENTS

        1. Example : Sati is not in Keeping with Modern Humanitarian Value ; By Raja Ram Mohan Roy
        2. RRMR - He Relied on Vedas, but was Rationale ; Like idea of Love of Christianity ; Rationale was at centre point

        REVIVALIST - ARYA SAMAJ, THEOSOPHICAL, DEOBAND - BACKWARD MOVEMENTS

        1. Dayanand Saraswati - He said Religion at source was pure, now impure ; Hence come back to Vedas that is Original

        SUMMARY

        1. Every Religion would have a Reformer & a Revivalist

      • SRRM Trends

        • Reforms from WITHIN
          1. Raja Ram Mohan Roy & Brahmo Samaj
        • Reforms through LEGISLATIONS
          1. Bentick - Sati Abolished - Raja Ram Mohan Roy
          2. Dalhousie - Widow Remarriage Act - Ishwar Chandra Vidyasagar
        • Reform through SYMBOLS OF CHANGE
          1. Young Bengal Movement - Initiated by Henry Vivian - Professor in Calcutta Hindu College - He Inspired his students to think along modern lines of European thoughts - Discarded Indian Religion Completely - Short Lived Movement
        • Reform through SOCIAL WORK - Most Efficient One
          1. Swami Vivekananda - Ramkrishna Mission - Social Work at Centre of Stage

      • SRRM Summary

        /Screenshot_2022-09-12_at_10.42.46_AM.png)

      • SRRM General Nature

        1. Reformation (Goal) not Revolutionist - Forward Looking
        2. Revivalist Tendencies - Couldn't be avoided
          1. Problem is talking about Glory of Ancient Past
          2. Each Religion had its own past of Glory - Hence chances for coming of Communal Sentiments in India
        3. Removal of Social Evils - Most Initiatives helped in Removal of Social Evils
        4. Urban Phenomenon - Most Reformers were from Cities & their followers were in Cities - Hence no Proper Organisational Structure or takers in Rural Villages - Where it was needed most because of more stringent orthodox sentiments in Village

      • SRRM Limitations

        1. Generally Localised & Isolated - No All India Character
          1. RRMY - in Bengal
          2. Other Reformers with Similar Vision were Separated by Space and Time
        2. Lack of Unity & Sound Organisation
        3. Overemphasis on Religion
        4. Tendency to Look Back - Revivalist Tendencies
        5. Neglect of Medieval Indian History - By Scholars, Historian was also neglected due to Communal Sentiments
        6. Constrained by Limitations Inherent to a Colonial Society - Britishers only allowed those changes which were beneficial to them
        7. Inability to Perceive the Exploitative Nature of British Rule

      • SRRM Significance

        1. Cleansing of Religion & Society - Caste System began to lose its hold
        2. Spread of Education - Particularly Education of Women
        3. Regained Confidence - Self Respect and Pride
        4. Rise of Rationale Middle Class with Modern Outlook - Most National Leaders Emerged from this Class
        5. New Values of Secularism & Democracy became Centre Stage, Imbibed into Minds of India, Enshrined in Constitution
        6. SRRM had a Limited but a CERTAIN Impact on People & Nation
        7. Hence they are also Called “Prelude to the Rights of Nationalistic Sentiments in India”

      Hence Socio Religious Reform Movements in India are called as Pre Political Phase of National Movement OR { Prelude to Nationalistic Sentiments in India }

      • Violent - Various Uprising & Tribal Movements

    • Resistance - Violent - Before 1857 & During Companies Rule

      • Basic Cultivation
        1. REBELLION - implies an open formidable resistance that is often unsuccessful
        2. UPRISING - implies a brief, limited and often immediately ineffective rebellion
        3. REVOLUTION - applies to a successful rebellion resulting in a major change
        4. REVOLT & INSURRECTION - imply an armed uprising that quickly failed or succeeds
        5. MUTINY - applies to group insubordination or insurrection especially against military authority
      • The Indian Resistance
        1. Establishment of British Rule - Slow Gradual Process Involving Wars and Conquests
          1. In this Process they had defeated and cheated many Indian Rulers - Subjugated Indian Rulers - Didn't Like British Attitude
        2. Forceful Subjugation of Indians - Resented & Resisted at Every Stage
        3. Rapid Changes by the British (Bureaucratic) - Adverse Impact on Various Aspects of Indian Life
        4. Two Fold Reaction - SRRM & Popular Uprisings
        5. First Expression of Protest against the Oppressive Foreign Rule
        6. Multitude of Uprisings - Civil Uprisings, Tribal Uprisings, Peasant Movements
      • Causes of the Pre 1857 Uprisings

        • Deposition of Native Rulers & Ruling Class

          1. By Fraud and Cheat
          2. Mysore, Maratha & Sikh Wars - Subsidiary Alliance Policy of Paramountcy & Doctrine of Lapse

        • Impoverishment of Scholarly & Priestly Class

          1. Priestly & Scholarly Class list their jobs and Positions in the Royal Courts
          2. Offended by Christianity and British Interference in Indian Religions

        • Exploitation & Impoverishment of Peasants

          • Due to Atrocious land Revenue Settlement of Britishers

        • Ruin of Indian Handloom and Handicraft Industries

          1. Destruction of the very economic factory of India
          2. Making India an Economic Colony of Britain - Rural Economy was Ruined
          3. Brits Destroyed Self Sufficient Economy of India

        • Destruction of Village Economy

          1. Famous for Brilliant Quality & Cheap Cost
          2. During Greater Mughals & Lower Mughals - Indian Textiles Dominated Europe
          3. Britishers wanted to stop this, they exposed heavy taxes on Export - High Tariff Policy
          4. Foreign Market Trade Stopped - High Tariff
          5. Local Market

            • Contractual Transactions & Spot Transactions

              1. Loss of Royal Patronage due to Deposing of Ruler who gave order earlier
              2. Now he took it to Local Market - where was British Saree which was much cheaper
              3. One way free trade - British Import Encouraged + Indian Export Discouraged
              4. Weaver - Lost Livelihood - Also the Cotton Maker & Forest Produce was ruined
              5. Now Cotton Seller - Started exporting to British, British Started making more Cheap Clothes and selling in India
              6. More and More Tried Entering Agriculture - Pressure on Agricultural Land Increased + Indian Agri Gamble on Monsoon

              /Screenshot_2022-09-12_at_11.47.49_AM.png)

          • Summary

        • Tribals Losing Hold Over Agricultural & Forest Land

          1. Most Tribals practise Shifting/Jhoom Cultivation
          2. Britishers wanted to Collect more land revenue, hence they were expanding zameendar land, hence tribal forest came in to use, tribals lost their forest
          3. Britishers introduced use of Reserved Forest - Particularly for Railways - Prevented tribal access to forest
          4. Also they were trying spread of Christianity within Tribals

        • Colonial Nature of Company’s Rule

          1. Highhandedness
          2. Racial Policies
          3. Economic Exploitation
      • Popular Uprisings
        • Civil Uprisings
        • Tribal Uprisings
        • Peasant Uprisings
      • Nature of the Popular Uprisings
        1. Aim to overthrow immediate oppressors
        2. Keen Ness to restore past conditions
        3. Socio Political Consciousness - Uproot the Political Source of Exploitation
        4. Popular Characters - Local People for Local Problems
          1. Characterised by Law & Order Problem → Transformed to Popular Uprisings
        5. Local Leadership
        6. Regional Extent - No Pan India Coverage of Uprisings
        7. Drew Strength from Ethnic Ties - Language Religion or Tribal Identity
        8. Unequal Nature of Warfare - hence could have been easily suppressed by British - Uprisings were not a Military Match to British
      • Limitation of Pre 1857 Uprisings
        1. Local Grievances
        2. Localised and Isolate - Separated by Space and Time - Not Coherent
        3. Traditional Outlook - Centuries old in form, ideology/cultural content
        4. No National Impulse or Common Efforts
        5. Pacification of Leaders by British through Concessions
        6. Indian War Fare was Outdated - Arms & Strategies Old
        7. Failed to Overthrow British Rule / Foreign Rule
      • Significance of Pre 1857 Uprisings
        1. First Expression of Protest against colonial rule
        2. Strong Tradition of Resistance to British Rule
        3. Prelude to the Watershed Revolt of 1857
        4. Served as huge inspiration during the National Movement

    Result - Revolt of 1857

    • Prelude

      /Screenshot_2022-09-13_at_10.35.22_AM.png)

    • Revolution v/s Revolt

      1. Revolution
        1. Rapid Significant & a Permanent Change in existing way of life
        2. Can be Political, Economic, Administrative etc
      2. Revolt
        1. Armed Uprising Particularly Against Government

    • Outline - Revolt of 1857 - “Red Lotus” Symbol of Revolt

      • Causes of the Revolt (PAE SEMI)

        • Political Causes

          • The Greedy Policy of Aggrandisement followed by Britishers
            1. To conquer as much territory as possible
            2. Wellesley’s - Subsidiary Alliance
            3. Hastings - Policy of Paramountcy - Policy of War & Intervention
          • Humiliation of Mughals
            1. Mughals made puppets of British Rulers - Shah Alam, Akbar II , Bahadur Shah Zafar
            2. Company Demanding Equal Treatment as Mughal Emperor - During time of Akbar II
            3. Heir Apparent of Bahadur Shah - Fakhruddin ; in Early 1850 Dalhousie promised Fakhruddin, that he would be emperor after Bahadur Shah Zafar
            4. Same year Canings became general - Fakhruddin Died in 1856, Next Heir Apparent was Mirza Mughal - who wanted confirmation from Canings on his emperorship
            5. Canning added to the Fuel
              1. Emperor wouldn't call himself an emperor
              2. they couldn't live in their palace
          • Dalhousie - Doctrine of Lapse
            1. Made a Point that states were annexed
          • Broken Pledges & Promises
            1. Nana Saheb - Maratha Confederacy
            2. Rani Laxmi Bai - Jhansi
            3. Begum Mahal - Awadh
          • Dethroned Rulers, Dependency on British, Collapse of Aristocracy

        • Admin Causes

          • Unsympathetic Administrators and their Policies

            1. European System in India not for welfare of India but for Exploitation of Indians

          • Oppressive Police Force & Their Atrocities

            1. European Police with unlimited Power had become an Oppressive arm of an state

          • Judiciary 1 - Bitter taste of Rule of Law

            PROBLEM 1

            1. Sympathetic towards upper caste/priestly caste - Preferential Treatment by Law
            2. Shudra Penny from Brahmin - Capital Punish && Brahmin Murder - No Capital Punishment
            3. Upper Caste has sense of Superiority over Lower until British
            4. Now Brahmins were considered second class by British

          • Judiciary 2 - Costly & time consuming judiciary

          • Rampant Corruption in British Administration

            1. Especially at Lower Level
            2. Corruption in India is a Legacy of British Rule

          • Racial Discrimination against Indians in Administrative Positions

          • Alien Nature of the British Rule - Absentee Sovereignty of British Crown

        • Economic Causes

          “The britishers had destroyed traditional economic fabric of India”

          • Conditions of Peasants & Zamindars
            1. Atrocious Land Revenue Settlements & Unprofitable Agriculture
            2. Dispossession of Zamindars
            3. Impoverishment of Peasantry
          • Conditions of Trading Class
            1. Promotion of one way free trade
            2. Indian Lost their Land and Foreign Markets
            3. No Protection / Support to Indian Industries - Deindustrialisation
          • Conditions of Artisans & Handicraftsman
            1. Lost Major source of Patronage
            2. Loss of Livelihood
            3. Lack of Alternate Sources of Employment except agriculture
          • Summary
            1. Heavy Taxations & Evictions
            2. Discriminatory Tariff Policy against Indian Products
            3. Destruction of Traditional Handicrafts - HIT peasants, Artisans & Small Zamindars
            4. Made Nature of Economic Colony - Dominance of British Capitalist who became rich due to Industrialisation controlled British Parliament
            5. we were made Exporter of Raw Material and Importer of Finished Goods
            6. India Turned into a Colonial Economy to Serve the British Capitalist Interests

        • Socio Cultural Causes

          1. British Policy of Social Exclusiveness - Sense of Racial Superiority - Administration with Racial Overtones
          2. Racial Superiority - Racial Arrogance - White Mans Burden - Dogs and Indians are not allowed
          3. Encouragement to Missionary Activities and Religious Conversions
          4. Support of British to Socio Religious Reforms
            1. Hindu Widow Re Marriage Act, 1856
            2. Abolition of Sati, 1829
          5. The Social Legislations on the Evils as Sati, Infanticide, Re Marriage of Widows etc were considered as interference in religion

        • External Causes

          1. Reverse Losses suffered by the British were confidence boosters to Indians
          2. Examples
            1. First Anglo Afghan War, 1839-42
            2. Anglo SIkh War, 1845-49

            • Crimean Wars, 1854-56

              Religious War b/w Turkey (Britain, France) & Russia

              Turkey Winner & Russia Defeated

              Britain also victorious - During War British Army was almost decimated by Russians, British Troops involved in Russia became free in 1856, Soldiers in Crimean War were sent to India to suppress the Revolt of 1857

        • Military Causes

          1. Long History of Military Uprisings
            1. Mutinies and Uprisings were not new - Generally Due to - Issue of Payments & Religious Sentiments
            2. Brutally Suppressed : Vellore & Barrackpore Mutiny
          2. Indian Soldiers in companies army were not happy → Discriminated in Matters of Position, Promotion and Salaries & Allowances
            1. Position - Indian Soldier wouldn't be promoted above Subedar ; this also would take him long time
            2. Salary - Served Double Work & Paid 1/3 Less Money
            3. Allowances - General Service Enlistment Act removed bhatta from the Foreign Deploys
          3. Before Dalhousie - Post Office Act 1854 took away postal privileges
          4. Hence, Conditions of the Service in the Army, Religious Sentiments were generally hurt by many means
          5. As Indian Soldiers helped britishers in expanding their territory → Missionary Activities within and with the Help of Army → Offended Indian Soldiers
          6. Annexation of Awadh - was shock to Indian Soldiers as 1/3 of army in eastern command was from Awadh Army.
          7. Indian Soldier, a Peasant in Uniform - Originally people in Army had peasant roots - their family and friends were being exploited by britishers - Army started asking questions

        • Immediate Causes

          1. Introduction of Enfield Rifle in January 1857
          2. When Ammunition was to be loaded in Rifle, Greased Paper Cartridge had to be beaten off
          3. This Greased Cartridge contained Cow & Pig Fat for prevention from Water Damage was the problem
          4. Protest Started in February 1857

      • Major Events & Course - Initial

        • Beginning and Spread of the Revolt

          1. Introduction of Enfield Rifle - January 1857
          2. Disaffection with the British Increased
          3. 19th Native Infantry 34th Native Infantry - Mangal Pandey 07th Awadh Regiment All Infantry was Disbanded
          4. Started in Feb 1857, by May 1857 Climax was reached

        • Meerut Episode - 3rd Native Cavalry - 9th May 1857

          1. 85 Soldiers Refused to use the rifle
          2. Military Court ordered prison for 10 years
          3. Next Day - Friends of these soldiers attacked Britishers, freed 85 soldiers
          4. Started Marching towards Delhi from Meerut

        • Revolt Began as a Sepoy Mutiny - 10th May 1857

          “It is said that, Revolt Started with a Humble Beginning in the form of Sepoy Mutiny due to religious reasons”

        • Why Did Soldiers march to Delhi ?

          1. Soldiers went to Delhi, Killed British Residents
          2. Bahadur Shah was persuaded and convinced to lead Indian Uprising
          3. Soldiers declared him emperor of India
          4. Bahadur Shah started writing to rulers → to form a Confederacy of Indian Rulers against britishers
          5. BSZ was a Nominal Leader → Actual Control was in hands of Court of Administration = 6 Military + 4 Civilians
          6. Prominent Personality was Bakht Khan

        • Two Unique Developments

          1. Expression of Profound Mughal Loyalty inspite of Mughals being weak
          2. Hindu Muslim Unity during revolt - Revolt under banner of muslim rulers and supported by Hindus - Common Enemy
            1. To Promote Hindu Muslim Unity - Cow Slaughter was banned in territory controlled by Rebellions

      • Supporters of British Rule

        1. Educated Middle Class
        2. Lower Class
        3. Social and Religious Reformers
        4. Zamindars and Money Lenders

          4. Raja Jagannath Singh/Rao invited Maulvi for meetign in his place Powayan

          5. No Help from Scindia ; they were defated in Battle of Gwalior ; Hugh Rose said had he helped revolt of 1957 would have been different
          6. Tantya Tope escaped again to Central India Forest ; Rani Died a Death of Soldier in June 1858

          3. Eastern UP was most affected Place during revolt ; 1.5 Lac people were killed ; 1 Lac casuality were civilians ; Hence General Again prevailed in people.

          1. PB Zameendars
          2. Middle Class
          3. PB Indian Rulers - Scindias

          1. Sikhs - Punjab
          2. Pathans - NWFP
          3. Gurkha - Nepal
          4. South Indians were Loyal to british too
          5. They helped becasue they lacked Nationalist Sentiments ; India didnt exist at that time
          6. Awadhis Soldiers were Loyal and

        • Medieval Arms and Equipments not a Military Match

          1. What weapons were used by Indians on British - Enfield Rifle
          2. Implying that Introduction of Enfield was not the real reason but the accumulated anger of Indians towards British
          3. Even if Enfield Rifle was introduced or not a Major uprising was bound to happen in the middle of 19th century

        • Uncoordinated & Poorly Organised - No Unified Ideology of Rulers

          1. BSZ - No Choice
          2. Maulvi - Jihad
          3. Nana Saheb - to be Peshwa
          4. Begum - Awadh

        • Absence of Modern Nationalistic Sentiments

          1. Lets Assume - Revolt of 1857 was succesfull - Britishers were out of Picture - Internal Enmity would have risen
          2. Would the British Gone - Modern Education & Means would be introduced by Indians ?

      • Nature of the Revolt

        💡 Analogy : Ane Kanndavada - Argument after seeing an Elephant

      • How will you describe the Revolt of 1857 ?

        The Revolt of 1857 was the first united Effort of many sections of Indian Society against the British for various reasons, though the Indian Effort failed to overthrow the foreign rule ; it contained elements of Anti Imperialism & the very seeds of Modern Nationalism which proved to be a huge inspiration during the subsequent stages of the Freedom Struggle.

    • Consequences : Positive Developments

      1. Government of India Act, 1858 - Act of Good Government in India.
        1. Abolished Company Rule & Started Crown Rule in India
      2. Allahabad Durbar, 1 Nov 1858 - Queens Proclamation, 1858 - By Queen Victoria - Set of Promises to Ruling Class and Common Please
      3. In Allahabad Durbar - Canings assumed the responsibility of Viceroy

    • Consequences : Negative Developments

      1. Increased Racial Animosity between the British & the Indians
      2. Setback to Social Reforms
        1. Especially Reforms of 1857 came to a Halt
      3. Muslim Renaissance received a set back
      4. Policy of Divide & Rule
        1. Between Castes, Religion, Region etc
        2. to fight within themselves
        3. forgetting the common enemy Britishers
      5. Creation of Perpetual misunderstanding between Hindus and Muslims
      6. Territorial conquest was replaced by Economic Loot - Era of Finance Imperialism encouraged in India

  • Phase 2 → Crown Rule Phase ( 1858 - 1947 )

    • Viceroys & Allied Developments

      • Viceroys’ (till Curzon) - Better Government Of India Act, 1858 (20)

        • Cannings (1858-1862)

          • Queens Proclamation, 1858 - ADMIN

            1. End of Era of Annexations / Expansions / Doctrine of Lapse
            2. Respect to the Dignity & rights of native princes subject to British Paramountcy
            3. Indian Rights, Customs & Practises to be given due regard while framing and administering law
            4. Equal & Impartial Protection under Law
            5. Freedom of Religion w/o British Interference
            6. Equal Opportunities in Government Services

          • White Mutiny, 1859 - POL

            1. Crown and Company ( Mixed - European + Indian)
            2. Company European - Lesser Experience & More Pay due to Achievement in India
            3. In 1858 Amalgamation Occurred
              1. Crown Army Cultural Difference
              2. Less Salary
              3. Didn't want the same ranks as that of Companies European Army
              4. Crown Army Rebelled
            4. Enlistment Bonus (2,800) or Go Back (10,000)

          • GOI Act, 1858 - Stanley First SOS - ADMIN

            • Evolution of GOI Act, 1858

              GOI Act Evolution.pdf

            • Contents of Act

              1. COD & BOC was abolished and replaced by Council of India
              2. COI was headed by Secretary of State, 15 Member Council
              3. SOS was a British Cabinet Minister - Under Parliament - Britain Constitutional Monarchy - Monarch could control SOS
              4. Until Now
                1. 60% Direct Under GG (British Indian Provinces) - Controlled By Governor General of India
                2. 40% Indirect by Alliances - Agent of British Monarch - Viceroy - Controlling Princely States
                3. Technically there should have been One GG and One Vice Roy but what if they fought ; would be detrimental to British
                4. Hence Same Person was given both the Positions
              5. Queens Proclamation Said - Era of Annexation had Ended ; But was under Paramountcy to the Crown
              6. Legislations made by GG & His Executive Council and other people put together applied to British Indian Provinces - Directly Ruled
              7. In Princely States - IF Viceroy Mandated ; Princely State Accepted - Then Only Apply
              8. First GG & Viceroy for India was Canning
              9. GOI Act 1858 was just an Administrative Makeover ; Applied to Only 60% of India
              10. For Name Sake : It was an act of Good Governance in India ; Rather than being exploited by a Company, Now you would be exploited by a Crown

          • Income Tax Act, 1860 - ECO

          • Drafting of IPC, 1860 - LAW

          • Indian Council Act, 1861 (1861, 1862, 1909(MM Reforms)) - ADMIN

            • Why Called Councils Act ?
              1. After Consolidating Control over indian territory they understood that Indians were to be added to the Administrative and legislative structure at national and provincial levels
            • Provisions (Common to All After 1861)
              1. Additional Membership of Indian Legislative Council was expanded
              2. Indianisation of Legislative Council
              3. Granting More Powers to Legislative Councils
            • Provisions
              1. Legislative Council was also established in newly conquered provinces like Punjab
              2. From 1861, some legislative powers to the provinces was given ; earlier all was with GG
              3. Hence ICA 1861 initiated Legislative Decentralisation in India
              4. If GG wanted to Promulgate an Ordinance( Emergency Provision) ; he was allowed to promulgate ordinances on his own ; earlier he needed majority vote of executive council
              5. Canning experimented with Administrative structures - Started giving Individual Responsibilities (Portfolio/department) to members of Executive Council
              6. This Portfolio system introduced by caning was officially recognised by this act
            • Parallel Development
              1. CPC, IPC, CrPC - Finalised - Implemented in late ?

          • Indian High Court Act, 1861 - LAW

          • Establishment of East India Railway, 1861 - MISC

          • Indian Police Act, 1861 - ADMIN

            Indian Police Commission under MH Courts

          • Enactment of IPC, 1861 - LAW

        • Elgin I (1862-1863)

        • John Lawrence (1864-1869)

          • Political Developments
            • Bhutan War, 1865 (Duar War)
              1. Cross Border Raids by Bhutan - Rulers used to Raid Indian territories frequently because of no clear boundary
              2. Britains Imperialist Ambitions
              3. Bhutan was a vital cog in the Indo Tibetan War - wanted good relation with Bhutan for good indo Tibetan trade
              4. John Lawrence initiated War with Tshewang Sithub and was forced to sign Treaty of Sinchula - Monetarily Compensated (50,000 Rs a Year), Peace with British & No Raid by them in India
              5. Commercial Viability of Duar Region for Supporting Tea Plantations
              6. Peaceful relations also implied - brits could now control Sikkim
            • Policy of Masterly Inactivity by John Lawrence towards Afghanistan
              1. The Masterly Inactivity was opposed to the policy of misdirected war and activity. As long as Russia was out of Afghanistan, the British remained inactive in Afghan affairs, but they had a hawk eye on affairs with readiness to strike if their interests were belied

        • Mayo (1869-1872)

        • North-Brook (1872-1876)

        • Lytton (1876-1880)

          • Political Developments
            • Policy of Proud Reserve
              1. It was aimed at having scientific frontier and safeguarding spheres of influence. According to Lytton, the relation with Afghanistan could no longer be left ambiguous
              2. Scientifically Established Boundaries - Between Afghanistan and India - to influence afghan trade and politics
              3. Sphere of Influence - Which you can influence indirectly both Politically and Economically
              4. But for this they had to have good relation with the ruler
              5. Afghan was not friendly with Britishers ; rather Sher Ali sided with Russia
            • Forward Policy
              1. This led to Second Afghan War
              2. Defeating unfriendly rulers ( Sher Ali ), installing puppet ameer (Yakub Khan)
              3. Yakub Khan forced to sign treaty with British called Treaty of Gandmak - British controlled defence and foreign policy of afghan - British residents appointed in Afghanistan
              4. British Resident in Afghanistan was killed by Afghan Tribe
              5. So, Yakub Khan Abdicated (Official resignation of king) from position of Ameer
          • Administrative Developments
            • Royal Titles Act, 1876
              1. Queen Victoria assuming the title of Kaiser E Hind or Queen Empress of India
              2. To Commemorate this Delhi Durbar was convened ( 1877, 1903, 1911)

        • DD Summary

          Summary

          British Royalty attended all the Durbar

          Monarch Attended only One Durbar of 1911

          1. DD 1877 by Litton - Queen Victoria Assumed Title

          2. DD 1903 by Curzon - New Monarch Edward 7

          3. DD 1911 by by Hardinge II - New Monarch George 5

            Decision taken in this Durbar of 1911

            1. George 5th Annulled / Abrogated the Partition of Bengal
            2. Announced the Shifting of British Capital from Calcutta to Delhi

        • Afghan Story (Timeline Jump)

          1. Abdur Rehman new ameer installed - tried balancing both Russian and British - Few Years passed
          2. Eventually Russians invaded Afghanistan in 1885 during times of Viceroy Dufferin (TIMELINE JUMP)
            1. Wanted to Capture Merv Oasis in Afghanistan and they reached as deep as a place called Panjdeh
            2. Hence Ameer Abdur Rehman had to fight against the Russian on his own
            3. Britishers now felt that if Russians went further Panjdeh, Russia could affect the British rule in India. Hence he gave a warning to Russia that if they exceeded further it would result in a full scale war ; they could keep the place before Panjdeh but not move forward to it
          3. Meantime Dufferin’s terms was over and he was Succeeded by Viceroy LansDown (TIMELINE JUMP)
            1. They appointed a Boundary Commission between Afghanistan and India
            2. Currently Durand Line is official boundary Between
              1. Pakistan and Afghanistan &
              2. India and Afghanistan - 111 KM ( in POK)
            3. Meantime Great Game was costing Russia and Brits a Lot ; Hence they wanted to Solve amicably the problem resulting in
          4. Anglo Russian Convention during times of Viceroy Elgin II (TIMELINE JUMP)
            1. Great Game in 1830 Bentick and Ended by Anglo Russian Convention in 1895
          5. Third Anglo Afghan War (1919-1921) during times of Viceroy Chelmsford
            1. Off Late Afghan got one opportunity to get rid of British control in World War 1
            2. Allied v/s Central Power - Britain was victorious but Britain was gravely week in terms of economy and military
            3. At this time the then ameer of Afghanistan tried pressuring British
            4. Britishers sent assassin and got Habibulla Khan was assassinated ; Amanullah Khan son of Habibulla Khan declared war on British and Hence Third Anglo Afghan War Occurred
            5. Hence British Signed Treaty of Rawalpindi (to define boundary) and Treaty of Peshawar (to accept independence) and Afghan became Independent in 1921
            6. First time it became Independent - Ameer of Independent Afghanistan was Amanullah Khan

        • Labour Welfare

          • FIRST FACTORIES ACT, 1881 - by Ripon

            Concerned with Welfare of Child Workers

            1. No Regulation of Child Labour Existed
            2. Many Accident and Deaths of Indian Labours used to Happen
            3. Factories Act of 1881 - First Ever Act in India for Labour Welfare according to which interest of young workers in factory was taken care of
              1. Children < 7 - Not Allowed to work in Factories
              2. Children b/w 7- 14 - Work only for 9 hours
              3. Children given - Mid Day Meal Break
              4. Children - 4 Holidays a Month
              5. Didn’t regulate Works of Men and Women
              6. Machines to be fenced, to prevent industrial accident
              7. Appointment of Supervisors to oversee the efficient implementation of the Act
              8. Applied only to factories having 100 workers or more
              9. Not work more than 4 Months in a Year

          • BOMBAY MILL HANDS ASSOCIATION, 1884

            1. By N M Lokhande ; First Labor Union of India

          • SECOND FACTORIES ACT, 1891

            Concerned with General Welfare of Workers

            1. Children Min Age from 7 to 9
            2. Children b/w 9 to 14 - 8 Hours Day Only with no Work at night
            3. Women Work at Night Prohibited - 2 Hours Rest allowed
            4. For All - Mid Day Meal Stoppage allowed
            5. For All - One Day a Week Holiday Allowed
            6. Applied to all factories employing more than 50 workers
            7. Working of Hours of Men wasn’t regulated

        • Famines of India

          Crores of Indians Died in Multitude of Famines that occurred in India

          • John Lawrence

            1. Famine Commission under Henry Campbell during Orissa Famine 1866-67

          • Lytton

            1. Famine Commission under Richard Strachey (1878) during Famines of ( Madras, Bombay, Mysore, Hyderabad, Parts of Central India & Punjab)

          • Elgin II

            1. Famine Commission under Lyall (1897) during Famines of 1896-1900
            2. Within 4 Years 1 Crore - 90 Lac Indians Died

          • Curzon

            1. 56 Akaal during times of Curzon ( 1956 according to Vikram Samvat (Saka) but according to European Calendar it was 1900 AD)
            2. Famine Commission under Anthony McDonnell (1901)
            3. Submitted in Same Year the Report but there was NO immediate remedy (No Official Assistance) rather a list of Long Term Capacity Building Measures (Non Official Assistance) like
              1. Prevention of Demoralisation of People
              2. Construction of more railways so that food can reach to affected areas more quickly
              3. Establishment of Agricultural banks and cooperative credit societies for distribution of advances and loans to the peasants
              4. Setting up Famine commissioner in famine affected provinces
              5. setting up of irrigation commission which needed time

        • Development of Press in India

          • Censorship Act, 1799 - Wellesley

          • Licensing Regulations, 1823 - John Adams

          • Metcalfe Act/Press Act, 1835 - Metcalfe

          • Licensing Act, 1857 -

            1. Applied to All Vernacular and Other Press in India
            2. It added licensing restrictions to the already existing registration procedure established by the Metcalfe Act and the Government reserved the right to halt the publication and distribution of any book, newspaper or printed matter

          • Registration Act, 1867 - John Lawrence

            1. Applied to All Vernacular and Other Press in India
            2. Its goal was to limit free speech by instituting several procedural requirements for anyone who wanted to start publishing a book newspaper or other work. In Fact, failure to follow these minor procedures rendered the entire publication illegal

          • Importance of Vernacular Press

            1. Vernacular Press was the most important carrier of Indian Nationalist Sentiments in India until the Nook and Crook of India to Villages

          • Vernacular Press Act, 1878 - Lytton - THE GAGGING ACT

            1. Publisher of a Vernacular Press had to deposit a Security money with the DM.
            2. The Owner of the Press Published “Seditious Articles in Newspapers”
            3. If Further contempt was made even after Security Deposit was dissolved. All the Press Equipments was to be confiscated by DM
            4. In Vernacular Press Act, there was no scope of appeal against the DM to High Court
            5. This Made Indian Journalists Innovative
              1. Amrit Bazar Patrika - Overnight they made it English Newspaper
              2. Continued Publishing Seditious News and in last would quote Courtesy of Some English Paper

          • Repeal of Vernacular Press Act - Ripon

          • Amendements to IPC, 1897 - Elgin II

            1. with 124 A addition of 153 A was done
            2. Section 124A defines Sedition as “Whoever, by words, wither spoken or written, or by sign, or by Visible representation, or otherwise, brings or attempts to bring hatred or contempt, or excites or attempts to excite disaffection towards, the government established by law shall be punished with Imprisonment for Life, to which a fine may be added.
            3. Promoting enmity between different groups on ground of religion, race, place of birth, residence, language, etc and doing acts prejudicial to maintenance of harmony

          • Important Press & Publishers

            3. was supporter of revolutionary activities, hence was arrested and sent to jail for 18 months and came back to be called LOKAMANYA
            4. Chapekar Brothers were disciples of Tilak

          • First to be Arrested

            1. James Augustus Hickey - First Journalists in India to be Arrested
            2. Surendranath Banerjee - First Indian Journalist to be Arrested

          • Official Secrets Act, 1904 - Curzon

            1. The Law meant for ensuring secrecy and confidentiality in governance mostly on national security and espionage issues
            2. One of the main purpose of the act was to muzzle the voice of nationalist publications

    • Foundation of INC - Extremists & Moderates

      • Indian National Congress - Pg 268

        • Introduction

          1. In times of Dufferin ; By A O Hume ; First President was W C Banerjee
          2. in Gokuldas Tejpal Sanskrit College in Mumbai ; Attended by 72 Indians and Europeans ; 28th December 1885
          3. Named by : Dadabhai Narojee
          4. First General Secretary : A O Hume
          5. First President : Umesh Chandra Banerjee or W C Banerjee
          6. Decided on Meeting on Yearly Basis : Called Annual Sessions of Congress

        • Annual Sessions and Rules - Handouts

          1. No Home Member of the Province could become a President of Congress.
            1. If Annual Session happened in a Province ; For Example ; Madras, he couldn’t contest for Congress President
            2. Hence this would have reduced India to a Regional Level Association
          2. was based on Majority of Votes.
          3. Any Proposal by President : Was generally accepted by the Majority of Congress but there are some exceptions

        • Three Day Annual Affair of Congress

          1. was the First National Political Level Associations
          2. but was not a Year Long working Association
          3. Annual Associations were the days they met and discussed ;
          4. Hence called that “ Congress in its Initial days was a three day annual affair”

        • Theories on Emergence of Congress - Pg 269

          • Safety Valve Theory - Wrong

            /Screenshot_2022-09-19_at_11.42.43_AM.png)

            1. Dufferin called A O Hume to Make Congress so that Indian Anger could be translated through and means of Congress
            2. Venting in Formal Manner → would reduced Revolutionary Potential of India fuelled by Indian Discontent of British Repressions
            3. Safety Valve theory says that the British had seen the Political Situation in the country leading to another rebellion on the lines of the mutiny of 1857 and they wished to avoid such a situation. So, they wanted to provide a platform to the people, where they could discuss their political problems. Indian National Congress was to work as a Safety Valve against the Popular discontent

            Is Safety Valve Theory Correct ?

            NO - REASON 1

            1. Letters exchanged between Dufferin and AO Hume in the archives present that Hume was Nagging Dufferin to allow Establishment of Indian National Congress
            2. Why Hume Said So
              1. Good Man
              2. Contacted by Sadhus in Himalayas for him to establish Indian National Congress

            NO - REASON 2

            1. Indians became politically active from 1830, INC was formed in 1885
            2. hence by accepting the Safety Valve theory you are demeaning the regional efforts of Indian Political Association for the Last 50 Years

          • Lightning Conductor Theory - Correct

            Analogy of the Theory

            1. Building - INC
            2. Lightening - Dufferin (would have suspected intentions of INC)
            3. Hence INC used A O Hume as a Lightening Conductor to prevent the NOT FORMING OF INC

          • Summary

            The Indian Leaders understood that of the Pan Indian Platform was established under the leadership of Some Indian Nationalists, the British would not allow it to survive. They Understood A O Hume Intended to use them for the Protection of the British Indian Empire. Nonetheless, they joined forces with hin in order to used A O Hume as a protective shield of lightening conductor for the safety of congress. The early congress leaders used Hume as a Lightning conductor that is as a catalysts to bring together nationalistic forces even if INC was disguised s a Safety Valve

        • Goals of INC - Pg 270

          • A Democratic, Nationalist Movement
          • Politicise & Politically educate people
          • HQ foe the Movement
          • Friendly relations amongst Nationalists
          • Develop and Propagation an anto colonial nationalist ideology
          • Popular Demands & Unification of the people
          • Develop and Consolidate a feeling of National Unity & Nationhood

        • Indian National Social Conference, 1887

          1. INC was not initially involved in Social Movements, hence some congressmen started using

          2. Not Established by Congress but by Congressmen M G Ranade and R Raghunath Rao

          3. Wherever Annual Session ; there was Session of Indian National Social Conference

          4. Was so hand in hand with session of INC that at one point of time it was called the Sister Concerns of INC

        • Moderates in INC ( 1885-1905) - Pg 275

          • Introduction

            /Screenshot_2022-09-19_at_12.09.02_PM.png)

          • Initial Pedagogy

            1. Were Educated Upper Class and Caste Indians
            2. Majority Moderates in beginning were believers of Providential Mission
            3. Initially they didn’t want to include masses in INC ; inclusion would divide the ranks of congress leading to differences of opinions ; hence they didn't had a large a social base due to not having inculcated masses.
            4. Uniting the Masses first ; then include the masses. Moderates wanted to introduce Educated Middle Class Indians into the Ranks of INC
            5. Pray Petitions Protests method - They chose to protect by the Limits of Law ; constitutional methods of protests.
            6. Moderates didn’t had popular support and hence there petitions weren’t hear by Britishers
            7. Some Say : they were comprador (Agents) of Britishers ; thats wrong they were nationalists and patriots

          • Contribution

            1. Initiated Methods to Bring - Constitutional & Propaganda in Legislature (Common to All INC Ideology until Independence)
              1. Expansion of Legislature ( Centre + Province )
              2. Indianisation of Legislature - Inclusion of More Indians in this Legislature
              3. Introducing Principle of Popular Representation
              4. More Powers to the Legislatures ; Especially the Budget (economic policy)
            2. Campaign for General Administrative Reforms
              1. Indianisation of Administration
              2. Creating a Pro Indian Administration
              3. Separation of Executive & Judiciary
              4. Making Judiciary More Efficient and Less Costly
              5. Reducing expenditure on Aggressive Foreign Policy & unwanted warfare ( Anglo Afghan War on Economic Tab of India)
              6. Improving Conditions of Indian Indentured Labours particularly in foreign colonies ( Caribbean to South East Asia)
              7. Increasing Budgetary Allocation to Public Welfare
            3. Demands Regarding Economic Reforms
              1. Protection to Indian Industries
              2. Encouraging Indian Capital in to the Economy
              3. Improving the Conditions of Agriculture
              4. Abolishing unjust Taxes and Duties
            4. Economic Critique of British Imperialism
              1. By Dadabhai
            5. Civil Liberties
              1. Freedom of Speech and Expression
              2. Freedom to form Association
              3. Freedom of Press

          • Analysis

            1. But none of the demands are related to socio religious reforms

            2. Though they didn’t had popular support ; Britishers wanted to engage with them properly because they were rich class of India

            3. Led to inculcation of Political Demands in ICA, 1892

          • Indian Council Act, 1892 - Pg 267

            • Summary

              /Screenshot_2022-09-20_at_10.49.35_AM.png)

            • Schematic Changes

              1. Executive Council - GG + 6 Members (Officers = Civil + Military → Appointed by British Govt - Appointed/Official Member)
              2. Additional Members - 10 to 16
                1. Some were official appointed by GG of India = Official Members
                2. Rest would be nominated by Viceroy/GG for term of 2 Years - Britishers (Ex : Capitalists) & Europeans & Indians (Raja of Banaras/Sir Dinkar Rao Earlier + Nomination by Popular Indian Body by ICA, 1892) = Non Official Members
                3. 1853 - 06 Additional Members
                4. 1861 - 06to12 Additional Members
                5. 1892 - 10to16 Additional Members
              3. 1 & 2 put together forms Indian Legislative Council in Central and Provincial Legislatures
              4. At Any Given point of Time - In Centre and Province - Officials > Non-Officials

            • Changes

              1. Expansion in Legislative Councils
              2. Popular Representation - Nomination of Non Official Members by Influential Bodies
              3. Indianisation of Legislatures
              4. More Powers to Legislatures
                1. Indian Members Could Ask ONE Question ; No Supplementary / Followup Questions ; No Discussion on Answers.
                2. But they could discuss the Budget ; they could discuss but couldn't vote even on the parts of budget (cuts in grant)

          • Resentment of Middle Class

            1. Educated Middle Class Indians in INC weren't happy with ICA, 1892.
            2. They resented means and questioned leaderships ; who later became Extremists or Militant Nationalists

            • No Official Majority
            • Limited Powers
            • No Regular Meetings
            • INC became Critical of British

              1. Indian Tradition + Christian Tolerance + Tolstoys Ideas = Satyagrahi Techniques
              2. Pillars of Satyagraha = Truth & Non Violence
              3. Gandhis Nephew = Magan Lal Gandhi - names this Technique as = Sadagraha - Firmness in Good Causes
              4. Gandhi Preferred - SatyaGrah in English Meaning - Truth Force (Closest Translation)
              5. Exact Meaning of Satyagrahi - A Person who would always try to uphold Truth and oppose injustice = Pg 342
                1. Never Bow before the Evil
                2. Truthful, Non Violence & Fearless
                3. Non Cooperation & Boycott
                4. Suffering, a Part of Love for truth
                5. No Ill Feeling / Hatred - Hatred is Alien to Nature of Satyagrahi
                6. Only for the Brave
                7. Violence was Preferred to Cowardice - Satyagrahi is not for the week hearted
                8. Violence committed in Self Defence and to protect ones own Dignity is allowed
                9. Satyagrah is a Choice ; Gandhi cant compel. He always wanted to stand on side of Justice
                  1. Allies (Britain) v/s Central Power - World War 1
                  2. Allies (Britain) v/s Axis Powers - World War 2
                  3. Satyagrahi Supported Allies because they were on the side of justice as they hadn't initiated the war
                10. Satygrahic Techniques used to secure rights of techniques in South Africa

            • Satyagrahic Campaigns - Pg 339, 340

              • Satyagrah Against Registration Certificates - by Asiatic Registration Ordinance, 1907
                1. Registration was to be maintained by only Non White People
                2. He initiated a campaign to burn the Registration Certification
              • Campaign Against Restriction on Indian Migration, 1908
                1. When Indians wanted to move from One province to other they had to get Migration Certificates
                2. Stood at Borders on both sides and took a step on the other side - now they violated the law, all the people were arrested.
                3. Now they all were in Jail ; overrun capacity of Prison ; to put pressure on Government
              • Campaign Against Imposition of Poll Tax on Indentured & Ex Indentured Migrant Labours, 1913
                1. Poll Tax was 60% of Income
              • Invalidation of Indian Marriages, 1913
                1. All Marriages except for methods of Christianity was nullified
                2. children bourn out of wedding was bastards ; onslaught on dignity of Indian Women
              • Protest Against Transvaal Immigration Act, 1913 - Specific to Transvaal
                1. Indians Immigrating into the province of Transvaal due to its prosperity

                1. Three Phases of Decay for any living & non-living entity
                  1. Growth
                  2. Maturity
                  3. Decline

              4. Gandhi Mass Movement had the Similar Phases

                /Screenshot_2022-09-22_at_11.06.57_AM.png)

                1. Growth
                  1. Ultimatum
                    1. Set of demands with a Prescribed Deadline ; If demands are not honoured within deadlines ; people involved in relationship is declined.
                    2. Meanwhile he used to Publish it in the Newspaper, people would find it correct and support it
                  2. Mass Support
                2. Maturity - Peak of Mass Support
                  1. Different People had different Capacities to participate and contribute in the movement
                  2. He realised people wouldn't participate forever ; the potent was not universally infinite
                  3. Because People had limited capacity ; when peak of mass movement was there ; Pressure on the government was put ; and hence at the same time Compromise would be accepted by him
                  4. Compromise : out of 10 if 5 were met, he would accept ; but not compromise with his ideology
                  5. He believed that a Leader of Mass Movement should be able to take Un-Popular Opinion despite resistance. Example being withdrawal of Non Cooperation after Chauri Chaura
                  6. Gandhi was a Dictator when it came to mass movements
                3. Decline
                  1. He had realised that mass movements were short lived ; and hence even if the compromise wasn’t accepted the Mass movement would decline

      • Montford Reforms & GOIA, 1919 - Pg 334
        1. Centre & Provincial Executive Council were ALL POWERFUL but NOT RESPONSIBLE
        2. Montford - Montague(SOS) Chelmsford(Viceroy) Reforms - Pg 336

        • Schema

          /Screenshot_2022-09-27_at_11.14.35_AM.png)

        1. GG / VR & His Viceroy

          1. GG was not responsible to Legislature - No Responsible Government in Centre

        2. Lists - Subject of Administration

          1. Central List - by Centre - { GG & EC of GG had Complete Authority }
          2. Provincial List
            1. Reserved - { GG & EC of GG had Complete Authority } - DEALT WITH POWER
            2. Transferred - by Provincial Ministers - DEALT WITH RESPONSIBILITY

        3. Central Legislature (CL)

          1. More than 100 Members in Central Legislature in Upper House are elected by DIRECT ELECTIONS with only 0.6% if Indians were Enfranchised or Given the Right to Vote (For Educated and Tax Paying Section)
          2. Who were these 0.6% - Hindu Upper Caste and Rich Sections of Society
          3. Ask Questions, Discuss Budget, Reject 25% of Budget,
          4. Bi Cameral Legislature
            1. Lower House (145 Member) - Indian Legislative Council
              1. Forum for Britisher Ruled 60%
              2. Elected Majority
              3. Term 3 Years
            2. Upper House - Council of States
              1. Forum for Princely States 40%
              2. Nominated Majority
              3. Term 5 Years

        4. Provincial Legislatures (PL)

          /Screenshot_2022-09-27_at_11.26.10_AM.png)

          1. Executive = Governor + Executive Council + Ministers from Provincial Legislative Council
          2. Provincial Governor and his Executive Council were not Responsible to the Provincial Minsters
          3. Provincial Minsters were responsible to the Governor & his Executive Council
          4. Provincial Legislators were Elected by Local Self Government.
          5. Life of Provincial Minsters were 3 Years
          6. Introduction of Diarchy in Provinces - Most Important

    • Nehru Report

      • Pre Lude to Nehru Report

        INC tried to Draft a Constitution & Nehru Report - All Party Conference Nehru Report / Draft Constitution - Dominion Status ( cuz minorities & depressed classes wanted the Britishers Rule to Continue )

        /Screenshot_2022-09-30_at_11.30.05_AM.png)

      • Nehru Report

        1. Nehru Report was only concerned with Constitution of British Territories

        2. Representatives of Princely State could sit together and draft constitution for themselves as a whole

        3. Hence, sought dominion status but a federal linkage view

        4. Proposed Broadly Unitary Structure - Centre control all in emergency

        5. Nehru Report Rejected Separate Electorate but wanted Join Electorate

        6. Reservation to Muslims

          1. 25% seats in Central Legislatures
          2. Reservation only in minority provinces

        7. Separation of Judiciary from Executive

        8. Proposed Linguistics Provinces

        9. Decision about new muslim major provinces to be taken after Britishers give dom status

        10. 19 Fundamental Rights & Equal Right for Women

        11. Safeguards to Muslims & Minorities

        12. Proposed that India should be a Secular Country - State to Disassociate itself from any Religion

        13. Legislative Reforms Proposed by Nehru Report

          1. There should be a Bi Cameral Legislature

            1. Upper House - Council of State - 200 - 7 Years
            2. Lower House - House of Rep - 500 - 5 Years

          2. Provincial Legislature - 5 Years

          3. Viceroy

            1. Could be Indian
            2. But had to work in aid and advice of CoM
            3. But he had to be paid through Indian Purse

        14. Conclusion

          Nehru Report Proposed a Central Government in Centre and State

      • Conflict of Interests

        Publication of Nehru Report had 2 Types of Reaction

        1. Reaction by Congress

          1. Goal - Poorna Swaraj
          2. Independence for India League
          3. President - Sri Nivas Iyengar

        2. Reaction by Jinnah

          1. Wanted a Few Amendments in Draft Constitution
          2. Muslims should get 1/3rd Seats in Central Legislature
          3. Muslims Should get reservation in Majority Provinces as well
          4. Residual Powers should be given to Provinces
          5. Congress Rejected all the Demands of Jinnah by 1928
          1. Hence, Jinnah withdrew his Approval given to Nehru Report
          2. He Joined the Mohammed Shafi Group

      • Quaid E Azam’s - 14 Points

        It was a Reaction to Congress not accepting the amendments proposed by Jinnah, that Jinnah proposed his 14 Points, saying that if these were not accepted, muslims would not support Nehru Report

        The Fourteen Points

        1. The form of the future constitution should be federal, with the residuary powers vested in the provinces
        2. All provinces must be given equal Autonomy
        3. All legislatures in the country and other elected bodies shall be constituted on the definite principle of adequate and effective representation of minorities in every province without reducing the majority in any province to a minority or even equality
        4. In the Central Legislature, Muslim representation shall not be less than one-third
        5. Representation of communal groups shall continue to be by means of the separate electorate at present it shall be open to any community, at any time, to abandon its separate electorate in favour of joint electorate
        6. Any territorial distribution that might at any time be necessary shall not in any way affect the Muslim majority in Punjab, Bengal Land NWFP provinces
        7. Full religious liberty shall be guaranteed to all communities
        8. No bill or resolution shall be passed in any legislature if three-fourths of the members of any community in that body oppose the bill
        9. Separation of Sindh from Bombay
        10. Reforms should be introduced in the NWFP and Balochistan the same footings as in the other provinces
        11. Muslims should be given an adequate share in all services, having due regard to the requirement of efficiency
        12. The Constitution should embody adequate safeguards for the protection of Muslim culture, education, language, religion and personal laws, as well as for Muslim charitable institutions
        13. One-third representation shall be given to Muslims in both central and provincial cabinets
        14. No change will be made in the constitution without the consent of the provinces

      • Bose & Indian National Army - Pg 494

        💡 2023 is ***80th Year of Subhash Chandra Bose***

        • The Initial Story

          1. Arrested & Imprisoned in Mid 1945
          2. Against Memorial Hall well
          3. Subhash Chandra Bose disguised himself as a Muslim Insurance Agent Ziauddin ; with the help of Bhagat Ram (was a spy for 5 countries) via Peshawar reached Afghanistan
          4. From Afghanistan he reached Russia
          5. but before he could reach Russia, Hitler invaded Russia
          6. Bose understood that USSR couldn't help him
          7. he went to Axis Powers, in Italy by the name of Orlando Mazzatto
          8. Bose asked Hitler to give him the India army captured so that it could be raised against the British
          9. Bose stayed in Germany (Dussel) and gave speeches to unite and call Indians to join Army
          10. Bose’s Secretary was Abid Hasan - Suggested to give a WarCry - He chose the Slogan Jai Hind
          11. Jai Hind was Coined by a South Indian living in Germany called Champaka Raman Pillai. He used to use his words in his telephonic conversation
          12. Here Japan was friends of Germany, hence with the help of Germany, Bose could launch an attack from South East Asia which was at that time under control of Japan
          13. In Japan he used a Identity - Abid Hussain ; Met Hideki Tojo
          14. Hideki Tojo → gave him the Indian army war soldiers to him for Raising an Army Against Britishers
          15. Japan asked Bose to go to Singapore in South East Asia
          16. Singapore was because ; similar minded people of Bose ; Mohan Singh created in INA 1942 with 1600 soldiers from war prisoners of India.
          17. Rash Bihari Bose in Japan had established India Club of Tokyo in 1925 - ICT was doing propaganda against Western colonial powers
          18. During WW2 - Rash Bihari Bose thought that it was time to free India so he established IIL - Indian Independence League, 1942 (IIL)
          19. Meeting of IIL, 1942 in September - Mohan Singh’s INA was placed under IIL
          20. Rash Bihari Bose liked Subhash Chandra Bose immensely
          21. Rash Bihari Bose happily transferred control of Indian National Army ; S Bose was made commander of Indian National Army by R Bose in South Easy Asia on 25 August 1943
          22. R Bose died 4 year Later due to ill health of lungs

      • Introduction

          3. Stafford Cripps

        2. Cabinet Mission Reached India with two Goals

          1. Short Term Goal

            Help Indians to form a Provisional Government

          2. Long Term Goal

            Help Indians to form a Constituent Assembly for a Constitution there by giving them Independence

        3. Frustration of Cabinet Mission Plan

          1. Because ML and Congress didn’t come to a common term with no matter what so ever efforts

        4. Hence, in frustration they announced the Cabinet Mission Plan

      • Cabinet Mission Plan, 1946

        • The Fear of Division

          Non Muslims in North Western and North Eastern Part of India

          Going by the Demand and Logic of Muslim League, now these Minorities would also later demand a Separate State

          1. demand of Khalistan by Sikhs
          2. leaders like Periyar wanted a South India of 4 states within a single country

          /Screenshot_2022-11-09_at_4.46.02_PM.png)

          Extension of this Logic would have led to demands for formation of many small states

        • The Provisions

          1. No Full Fledged Pakistan

            Due to Political, Economic, Social, Military & Strategic Nations

          2. CA in Indian would have 389 Members

            1. 292 - British Provinces
            2. By 11 Provinces divided
            3. Indirectly Elected by Provincial Legislatures
            1. 4 - Chief Commissioners Provinces
            2. would be Nominated by Vice Roy
            1. 93 - would be from Princely States
            2. Nominated by Prince of Princely States

            Hence, CA = Party Indirected + Partly Nominated

          3. But Bringing them to Consensus would be difficult

            Hence CMP 1946 proposed

        • Grouping of Constituencies

          1. Section A : Hindu Majority Provinces in India 6 Provinces

            There would be 6 Provincial Constitutions and 1 Group Constitutions

            Hence, there would be a Provincial or State Legislature

          2. Section B : Muslim Majority Provinces in North Western Part of India 3 Provinces

            There would be 3 Provincial Constitutions and 1 Group Constitutions

            Hence, there would be a Provincial or State Legislature

          3. Section C : 2 Provinces

            There would be 2 Provincial Constitutions and 1 Group Constitutions

            Hence, there would be a Provincial or State Legislature

          4. Note

            1. These 11 Provinces and their Provincial Legislatures and to elect 292 Members
            2. Initial Consensus at Individual Level
            3. Then Consensus at Group Level
            4. Then Consensus at Union Level or National Level

          5. CMP, 1946 envisaged a Three Tier Legislature & Executive

            1. Tier 1 - Provincial Legislature
            2. Tier 2 - Group Legislature
            3. Tier 3 - Union Legislature

          6. Division of Power

            Union Govt would control only Defence Commerce and Foreign Affairs

            Residual Powers would be with the provinces

          7. Hence, CMP 1946 proposed a

            Three Tier Structure Weak Union Strong Provinces Federal Structure

            /Screenshot_2022-11-09_at_11.56.51_PM.png)

          8. Same Applied to the Princely States as well

            /Screenshot_2022-11-09_at_11.58.50_PM.png)

          9. While Drafting the Constitution, there would be many communal questions which would be solved through Simple Majority in the Parliament

          10. Who Would Run the Interim Govt ?

            The members of Constituents Assembly would elect the members of Interim Government

          11. The Grouping of Indian States was for convenience

            After the New Constitution was Implemented, General Elections Occurred and New Govt Installed, a Province was free to come out of a Loop

            It could come out of a Group - Immediately

            Can consider for Reconsideration - After 10 Years of Election

          12. Attlee told no Veto to Minority? Congress Perceived to be Optional

            Cabinet Mission suggested Grouping was compulsory but temporary

          13. The Final Concept and Issue with Grouping of Cabinet Mission Plan

            1. Congress - Considered Grouping to be Optional

              Because it wanted NEFP to be in Group A cuz it wanted implementation of GOI Act 1935 provision of provincial autonomy

            2. Muslim League - Wanted it to be Compulsory

              For ML Section B and Section C was Muslim Provinces

            3. CMP, 1946 → Grouping though temporary was compulsory

          14. ML expected the Congress to reject the cabinet mission plan but

          15. Believing that Congress would reject the Plan, ML accepted the Plan

          16. Acceptance of Cabinet Mission Plans

            1. ML accepted Cabinet Mission Plan, 19 June 1946
            2. Congress accepted Cabinet Mission Plan, 24 June 1946

          17. Election Conducted - Constituent Assembly was Formed

          18. Why Did Congress Accept the Plan

    • INC v/s League

      • JLN Statement on 10 July 1946
        1. Constituent Assembly is a Sovereign Body within the Constituent Assembly, NWFP & Assam would have objection to Grouping
        2. Objection → Voting → Congress would have win
        3. Congress was Ruling 9/11 Provinces
        4. Because ML didn't had Majority in CA → Jinnah Rejected Cabinet Mission Plan
      • Direct Action Aug 16, 1946 - within 3 days of direct action
        1. Launched from Bengal
        2. 4000 Died and One Lac of Houses Burnt
        3. Muslims in India will either have a divided or destroyed India
        4. Communal Violence was Not Subsiding
      • British Fear
        • Britishers feared that all this blood will be in their hand, they wanted to leave as soon as possible
      • Wavell Invited Congress Ministers - Pg 495-496
        • Invited Congress to form Interim Govt
        • Congress Ministers joined Interim Govt in Sep 1946
        • ML was admitted into Interim Govt in Oct 1946
        • Leader of ML in Interim Govt was Liyaqat Ali Khan - Finance Minister of Interim Govt
      • Formation of Interim Govt
        • First Govt on 9 December, 1946
        • Muslim League Boycotted Constituent Assembly
        • Interim Govt - INC + ML
          • with a Swarajists Agenda - end/mend the Govt )
          • Congress thretaned Wavell that they will resign if ML Ministers dont cooperate
        • Constituent Assembly - Dominated by INC
        • Constitutinal Deadlock - Cainet Mission Plan had become a Dead Horse
      • Objective Resolution by JLN
        • later became inspiration for preamble of Constitution JLN passed historic resolution
    • Independence & Partition

      • Attlee’s Statement - Class Notes

        1. By 30th June 1948, britishers will Quit India, no matter what.
        2. When Britishers will quit India, if Indians don’t arrive at a Compromise, British Government will transfer power to anyone available → Hinting Towards Balkanisation of India
        3. Creation of 100’s of Countries in India
        4. Intention Good
          1. For Constitutional Deadlock be resolved
          2. Wanted to show that British were serious about leaving India
          3. It was not safe for Britishers in India ; Govt power had eroded beyond redemption
        5. New Viceroy - Mountbatten will replace wavell

      • Attlee’s Statement - Summary

        /Screenshot_2022-10-05_at_10.38.14_AM.png)

      • Why was it a Shock to both ?

        • Congress - Inspite of 100 have 2 countries
        • ML - chances of getting or not getting Pakistan

      • Mountbatten Condition

        /Screenshot_2022-10-05_at_10.40.45_AM.png)

        1. Knew that Cabinet Mission Plan had failed
        2. Congress & ML was in Shock

      • JLN Statement, March 10 1947

        1. Congress was ready to divide India but Bengal and Punjab Hindu Population

      • Kriplani Statement, April 1947

        1. Chairman of Congress - Kriplani Gave Official Statement inspite of War, let there be War

      • Deadlock Again

        1. The Proposal of Partition, had to be passed by ML & Congress in Constituent Assembly

      • Mountbatten Plan, 1947 / Dicky Bird Plan / 3rd June

        1. V P Menon, Secretary of Mount Bateen, ICS Officer, Suggested

        2. Despite bringing them at a common stage

        3. Each of them could take decisions regarding their territories

          1. Suggestion : Create Two Dominions & Two Constituent Assemblies along with the Right to Cession from British Empire.

        4. Creation of these 2 Dominions was Conditional

          1. Provincial Legislative Assembly of Punjab & Bengal will take decision regarding Partitions of their Provinces

          2. Legislatures of Punjab & Bengal will be divided into 2 Groups

            1. Muslims
            2. Hindus

          3. If at least one of the group voted in favour of Partition, there will be partition

          4. The Maths

            /Screenshot_2022-10-05_at_10.47.38_AM.png)

        5. Since, Congress accepted Partition, Dicky said that all demands of Congress will be met

          1. Princely States either Join India or Pakistan ; if they wouldn’t accept this → they would be treated as Enemy States → In Practicality
          2. Osmanistan joining Pakistan - Possibility Ruled Out
          3. Punjab and Bengal Independence - Practical Possibility Ruled Out

        6. Boundary Commission - 2 Under Same Chairman - Cyril Radcliffe

      • Indian Independence Act, 1947 - Pg 514-515

        1. Got Accent of George, the 6th
        2. According to IIA - India and Pakistan became Independent
        3. Pakistan Created
          1. First GG : Jinnah
          2. First PM : Liaqat Ali Khan
          3. Divided in : West & East Pakistan
        4. India Created
          1. First GG : Mountbatten
          2. First PM : JLN
        5. Congress Accepted Dominion Status in 1947
          1. To Curb Communal Violence
          2. Existing Financial Resources & Army could be divided
          3. Existing Bureaucratic Machinery could be used
        6. Even Before Physical Partition of India Happened, Mental Divide of Hindus & Muslims had already occurred
    • Gandhi - The Legacy
      • Gandhis view on Partition
        1. Gandhis Said : Physical Partition has become a Reality, but dont be divided in mind
        2. Gandhi was in East Pakistan ( Bengal )
      • Why is Contribution of Gandhi in essence ?
        1. With Gandhi Mass Movements & techniques ; He brought multiple sections of society on the common Platform
        2. Despite of 100s of Countries, India ended up having only 2 States
    • Independence & Contemplation

      • Dominion Status but Still Independent

        1. V P Menon Suggestion : Create Two Dominions & Two Constituent Assemblies along with the Right to Cession from British Empire.
        2. But we exercised this right only on 26th June 1950 and India became a SDR - Sovereign, Democratic, Republic

      • Who Should Get the Credit for Independence ?

        1. Independence of India was a result of Combined Efforts of People, Leaders & Circumstances
        2. Its the tale of Sung & Unsung heroes of Indian Independence
